# Changelog archive — 2026 H2 (July–December)

Full write-ups, newest first. The one-line index lives in [`../../CHANGELOG.md`](../../CHANGELOG.md);
the incidents worth reading on their own are in [`../incidents/`](../incidents/README.md).

<a id="2026-10-07-imagesharp-removed"></a>

## ImageSharp removed; images through SkiaSharp — backend, security — 2026-10-07

**Why.** On 2026-10-07 GitHub published five advisories against SixLabors.ImageSharp 3.1.12
(GHSA-j3p4-wp97-rph4, GHSA-j9gm-c75j-xc9q, GHSA-jjfr-hcj7-qf5w High; GHSA-gwg2-r3hj-4w44,
GHSA-wmxv-xphr-5c9g Moderate), and CI's "Vulnerable packages" step failed every PR. The only patched
version is 4.1.2. v4 keeps the same Split License but enforces it at build time: without a licence
key a Release build fails ("No Six Labors license found"), and every image is a Release publish. CI
builds Debug, where it is only a warning, so a bump would have gone green and broken the deploy.

**Exposure, read per advisory.** The three Highs (HistogramEqualization, the TIFF CCITT T4/T6
*encoders*) were unreachable: we never ran that filter and only ever wrote WebP. The two Moderates
were reachable, because `Image.Load` sniffed any format from uploaded bytes: an ICC profile could
force a huge allocation, and a BigTIFF disguised as an avatar could spin a decoder thread forever.
Denial of service, not data or code execution.

**What changed.** ImageSharp had two call sites. `ImageOptimizer` (avatars, admin covers, covers taken
from uploaded and catalog books) and `PdfTextExtractor.IsDegeneratePng` now use SkiaSharp 4.150.1 —
the version PDFtoImage already ships to the Worker, so the Worker image gains nothing and the Api image
gains the same musl `libSkiaSharp.so` (NoDependencies build: libstdc++/libgcc only, both in the base).
`ImageOptimizer` reads the header with `SKCodec` first and refuses anything above 50 MP before a
pixel buffer exists; Skia has no TIFF decoder. That check runs only where decoding happens — on
images over the 200 KB budget; smaller files are stored untouched, as before. Formats ImageSharp read
and Skia does not (TIFF, TGA, PBM, QOI) now fail to optimize: the catalog paths already skipped such
an image, and the user-upload cover path, which let the exception fail the whole book, now skips just
the cover (code review). Animated GIF/WebP over the budget keep their first frame only. Dependabot's "ignore ImageSharp majors" rule went with
the package.

**Tests.** Neither path had a direct test. `ImageOptimizerTests`: small image untouched, a ~1 MB noisy
PNG comes out as decodable WebP ≤ 200 KB, TIFF refused, a 45-byte PNG header claiming 30000×30000
refused by the pixel limit. `DegeneratePngTests`: transparent → degenerate, opaque → not,
undecodable → not. The PDF image regression test moved to Skia unchanged in what it asserts.
<a id="2026-10-07-web-bundle-on-github"></a>

## Web bundle built on GitHub, scanned before it is live — infra, docs — 2026-10-07

Review P2-2. The deploy built the web frontend on the production host: `pnpm install` (with
esbuild, puppeteer's Chrome download and `@sentry/cli` postinstalls running as the deploy user, who
has Docker) and `vite build` straight into the served `apps/web/dist`. The secret scan ran after that,
so a hit stopped the deploy with the bundle already live. It was also where the 76-minute corepack
hang and the asdf-shim failures happened.

**Now.** `images.yml` has a second job, `web`, beside `publish`. It installs the workspace (frozen
lockfile, no Chrome), sets every name the server's `.env` can hold (`.env.example` + compose
`${VAR}`s, the image canary list) to `CANARY_SECRET_<name>`, sources `apps/web/production.env`, builds,
scans the whole `dist` and pushes `ghcr.io/mrviduus/textstack-web:<sha>` — a `FROM scratch` image
carrying only `/dist` (no base, so no Docker Hub on this path). Its digest is a new `images` output,
`web_digest`. The deploy pulls `textstack-web@digest` before anything else, copies `/dist` out with
`docker create` + `docker cp` into the runner's temp dir, scans it again with the server's real
`*SECRET`/`*PASSWORD`/`*TOKEN`/`*API_KEY` values, and only then runs `scripts/swap-web-dist.sh`.

**Why an image, not an Actions artifact.** It reuses everything the six service images already have:
pull by digest (the bytes that passed the scan), "already published" for a rollback to a deployed SHA,
`ghcr-retention.yml` (one more name in `PACKAGES`; the script now skips a package that 404s, i.e. not
published yet), and `Cleanup old images` on the server. An artifact would have needed `actions: read`
on the self-hosted job, its own retention (90 days), and a rebuild path for rollbacks.

**The swap.** `mv new dist` was out: ssg-worker bind-mounts `apps/web/dist`, and a bind mount holds
the directory, not the path — after a rename it would write SSG pages into the old tree. So the release
goes in file by file: assets first (content-hashed, unreachable until named; an existing name is the
same bytes), every other file copied next to its target and renamed over it, `index.html` last; files
the previous release shipped and this one does not are removed (list in `apps/web/.web-release`,
outside the served tree); assets referenced by nothing are pruned. The keep set is the new release,
the previous `index.html`'s graph (one release of grace for open tabs — the old path had none) and
every `dist/ssg*` page's graph, walked HTML → JS → lazy imports → CSS → `url()` to a fixed point like
the SSG snapshot step (the HTML-only prune of 5e4070a deleted every lazy chunk). If live HTML exists
but the walk finds nothing, the prune is skipped with a warning rather than trusted. `dist/ssg*` is
never touched, so the SSG snapshot and the vite wipe it guards against do not happen on this path. The
restore step still runs on both paths: it is also what puts back `dist/ssg` after a deploy that died
between snapshot and restore.

**Fallback.** No `web_digest` (the job failed, or a rollback to a commit with no
`apps/web/production.env`), a failed pull or copy → `::warning::` and the old server path: Node check,
SSG snapshot, `pnpm install` + `vite build`, restore, scan (after the fact, as before). The fallback
now also writes `apps/web/.web-release`. Its `VITE_*` values come from the same file, taken from the
workflow's commit (as the scanner and the swap script are), so a rollback still builds.

**`VITE_*` values.** The code reads four: `VITE_API_URL` (`/api`), `VITE_STORAGE_URL` and
`VITE_CANONICAL_URL` (`https://textstack.app`), `VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` (the public OAuth client id).
All were inline in `deploy.yml`; none is secret. They moved to `apps/web/production.env` — not
`.env.production`, which vite would read in every build, CI and e2e included, and which the deploy
moves aside on the server. No repo variables: a missing variable would build a bundle with no Google
sign-in, and a file in git is reviewed like code.

**What the comparison found.** A Linux (`node:24.20.0`, amd64) and a macOS build of `d18f2244` with
these values and the canaries are byte-identical (same four entry hashes) and contain no canary. The
bundle live from that very commit is not: `react-vendor` carries react **19.2.6** and the sanitize
chunk DOMPurify **3.4.3**, while `pnpm-lock.yaml` has only react 19.2.3 (since the Expo 57 change) and
DOMPurify 3.4.16. The server's `node_modules` holds leftovers `--frozen-lockfile` never replaced, and
production has been shipping them. The `VITE_*` values are identical in both bundles (`/api` ×2,
`textstack.app` ×17, the client id). So the first pulled deploy changes production's React patch
level down to what CI tests and DOMPurify up to the locked version; that is the point of the move. The
stale server `node_modules` only matters to the fallback and is in `docs/STATUS.md`.

**Verified.** actionlint (pinned digest `sha256:887a259a…`, v1.7.7): 54 findings on main, the same 54
on the branch. `scripts/swap-web-dist.test.sh` (macOS and `debian:13-slim`): two releases over a fake
dist with `ssg/` and an in-flight `ssg-new/`, SSG trees byte-identical after, grace and prune right,
refusals leave `dist` alone, and a reader racing 30 swaps never saw a torn `index.html` or one naming a
missing asset. On the real bundle (27 assets) with an SSG page: two swaps of a tiny release keep all
27 (lazy chunks included) through the SSG graph and remove the 26 public files of the old release.
Now in CI (`frontend`, "Web swap self-check"). `pnpm test:scripts` green.

`images.yml` dispatched on the branch (run 37680404752): `web` took **52 s** next to `publish`'s 6 min;
`dist` 3.9 MB, scan clean (54 files); pushed `textstack-web@sha256:bd3b6604…` — one layer, 1.3 MB
compressed, no command, no env but PATH. Pulled and copied out exactly as the deploy does
(`docker create … /none` + `docker cp`): `diff -r` against the local Linux build is empty, and the
package is pullable anonymously (public by default, like the others). `ghcr-retention.yml` dry run on
the branch: `textstack-web` listed, 1 version, kept.

**Owner steps.** None: no repo variables (the values are in the file), the package came out public,
and the deploy logs in with `GITHUB_TOKEN` anyway. Optional: delete the server's stale `node_modules`
so a fallback build matches the lockfile.

<a id="2026-10-07-migrator-ef-bundle"></a>

## Migrator image: EF Core migrations bundle, ~1.4 GB → ~89 MB — infra, docs — 2026-10-07

The migrator was the last image still shipping the full .NET SDK, the backend source and a global
`dotnet-ef`, and it rebuilt the Api at every start before migrating. 1.40 GB compressed
(`textstack-migrator:10f164c2`, amd64), 3.97 GB on disk — the biggest pull of every deploy, for a
container that runs for seconds.

**Now** (`backend/Docker/Migrator.Dockerfile`): the SDK stage runs on `$BUILDPLATFORM` and produces
`dotnet ef migrations bundle --self-contained -r linux-musl-<arch>` (the `TARGETARCH` mapping the
Api/Worker images use) with Infrastructure as both migrations and startup project —
`AppDbContextFactory` is there, so the Api's own dependencies stay out. The runtime stage is
`runtime-deps:10.0-alpine` (digest-pinned, in Dependabot's `/backend/Docker` lane) + `krb5-libs` +
`postgresql-client`, running as the image's non-root `app` user. 88.7 MB compressed, 303 MB on disk;
the bundle itself is 192 MB uncompressed (self-contained runtime + Application's dependency closure).

`dotnet-ef` is installed at the version of `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Design` in
`Directory.Packages.props` (it was unpinned "latest"), so a bump moves both.

**`migrate.sh`, same behaviour:** the bundle is `database update` and nothing else — it cannot list
migrations. So the image's migrations are listed at build time (`dotnet ef migrations list
--no-connect` → `/app/known-migrations`, the build fails if empty), and everything the script used
`migrations list --connection` for is that file against `__EFMigrationsHistory` via psql: step 0
(applied but not in the image), the pre-migration pending list, and the post-migration
applied/pending state. One improvement: an unset `ConnectionStrings__Default` now exits 2 at once —
without `--connection` the bundle would fall back to the design-time factory's localhost default.
psql failures (bad password, unreachable DB) exit 1 before the bundle runs. The connection string is
never printed and never in argv: the bundle reads it through `AppDbContextFactory`'s
`CONNECTION_STRING`, psql through `PG*` variables (`ps` during a run shows `/app/efbundle` alone).

**Checked locally** (scratch compose project, amd64 images, fresh volume, Api image of `10f164c2`):

| check | result |
|---|---|
| fresh volume → head | exit 0, 143 applied; `/health/ready` 200, `schema: ok` |
| `run --rm -e MIGRATE_TARGET=<previous> migrator` | exit 0, 1 reverted; `/health/ready` 503, `schema: behind`, the one migration pending |
| re-run without a target | exit 0, 1 applied; `schema: ok` |
| planted unknown history row + target | exit 1, `ERROR: … 1 applied migration(s) this image does not contain` |
| same row, no target | exit 0, `NOTE: …`, nothing changed |
| unknown target name | exit 1, `The migration 'NoSuchMigration' was not found.` |
| wrong password / no connection string | exit 1 (psql) / exit 2 |
| canary `.env` + `scripts/scan-image-secrets.sh` | clean (7 layers) |

arm64 build also succeeds (`linux-musl-arm64`). Globalization: `runtime-deps` alpine is invariant
mode, like the Api's `aspnet` alpine image; the migrations ran unchanged on a fresh database.



## GHCR retention: prune old images, never the deployed ones — infra, docs — 2026-10-07

Every deploy publishes six images (`ghcr.io/mrviduus/textstack-{api,worker,admin,ssg-worker,migrator,mcp-server}:<sha>`,
ADR-020); at ~10 merges a day that is a few hundred versions a week. The packages are public, so
storage is free — this is tidiness, not cost.

**What runs.** `.github/workflows/ghcr-retention.yml`, Mondays 05:17 UTC and on demand.
`workflow_dispatch` defaults to `dry_run: true` and takes `keep_newest` / `keep_days` (floored at 5 and
1). Only the schedule or a manual run with dry run unticked deletes; any other event lists only. The
rules live in `scripts/ghcr-retention.mjs` (pure planning functions + tests in
`ghcr-retention.test.mjs`); the workflow only passes env.

**What is kept, per package.**

| reason | rule |
|---|---|
| deployed | a tag is the SHA of one of the last 5 successful `deploy.yml` runs, or of **any** deploy run since the oldest of them (failed and in-flight too: a run that failed after `compose up` left its SHA live) |
| recent | younger than 14 days (also covers a push still in progress) |
| newest | among the 10 newest tagged versions |
| other-tag | any tag that is not a 40-hex SHA |
| child | a manifest that a kept version's index references |

Everything else is deleted, tagged before untagged, oldest first, at most 150 per package per run.
Each deletion is logged with its version id and tags; the job summary has a table per package.

**How it knows what is live.** From the Actions API, not the server: the deploy runs of the last 30
days. A rollback deploys `rollback_commit`, not the run's `head_sha`, and the runs API does not return
inputs — so `deploy.yml` now has `run-name: Rollback to <sha>` on a rollback (default title
otherwise), and the script reads that title (7–40 hex only) and resolves it to the full SHA. An
unresolvable ref from an unsuccessful run is skipped (the guard rejected it, nothing deployed); from a
successful run it fails the job.

**Fails closed.** The plan for all six packages is made before the first delete. Any API error until
then — deploy runs (including a page count that does not add up to `total_count`), a package listing,
a registry token or manifest — exits 1 having deleted nothing, as does finding no successful deploy.

**Untagged versions.** Today there are none: `images.yml` pushes single-platform manifests from the
local Docker store, so each version is one tagged manifest. If that changes (multi-arch, buildx
`--push`, attestations), an index's platform and attestation manifests appear as untagged versions,
and deleting them breaks pulls of the tag. So an untagged version is deleted only if no kept version's
index references it (the script reads each kept index from `ghcr.io/v2/…/manifests/<digest>`) and it
is older than 14 days. The simpler rule — keep untagged versions younger than the oldest kept tagged
one — was rejected: an index's children are pushed seconds *before* it, so the oldest kept index's
children fall just outside that line.

**Why not `actions/delete-package-versions`.** It has no age rule, no dry run and no deletion cap; it
cannot protect a set of SHAs computed at run time except through one regex, and its untagged mode
does not check whether a still-tagged index references the manifest. A script against the REST API is
~200 lines with its rules under test. No new action, so nothing to add to the allowlist.

**Token.** `GITHUB_TOKEN`; the job has `packages: write`, `actions: read`, `contents: read`, the
workflow `permissions: {}`. GitHub grants the publishing repository the admin role on a package a
workflow published, and admin can delete. Listing (versions, manifests) is verified by the dry runs;
the delete call itself is not — it is exercised for the first time by the first scheduled run, and a
failure there fails the job (the versions stay). GitHub calls deleting via REST from a workflow
"public preview". The endpoints are `users/<owner>/…`: an org-owned repo would need `orgs/<org>/…`.

**One surprise.** Under `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GET …/workflows/deploy.yml/runs?status=success&per_page=5`
returned five runs from May and September, not the latest — twice, in different orders — while the
same call with a user token was correct. The script now lists every run of the last 30 days and sorts
them itself.

**Dry run, 2026-10-07** (run on the branch): 59 versions across six packages, all kept — the packages
are a day old, so the 14-day rule covers everything. Live SHA `d27bb765…` and the four deploys before
it are tagged in every package. With the window forced to 0 days and keep-newest to 5 (dry run only),
29 would go (api 7, worker 7, ssg-worker 6, mcp-server 4, admin 3, migrator 2); every deployed SHA was
still kept. Reproducible builds share digests: one migrator version carries the tags of five commits,
and keeping it for any of them keeps it for all.

**Review fixes (same PR).** Deploy runs are de-duplicated by id before the `total_count` check (a
duplicate on one page could hide a run missing from the other); runs are ordered and windowed by
`run_started_at` (then `updated_at`, `created_at`), so a re-run of an old rollback counts as recent;
fewer than 5 successful deploys in the 30-day window now fails closed, as the comment said — 30 days is
GitHub's re-run limit, so a wider window would add nothing a re-run can reach, and a month that quiet
pushed almost nothing to prune. No rollback dispatch ran after images were first published (the last
dispatched deploy was 2026-03-25), so no pre-`run-name` rollback target needs protecting. Known race:
a rollback started after a retention run listed the runs can lose its version mid-flight; the deploy
then builds on the server.

**A rollback past retention** still works: `images` finds the SHA missing, builds and publishes it for
the rollback ref (or the server builds it). Slower, not blocked.

<a id="2026-10-07-ssg-followups"></a>

## SSG follow-ups: crawlers get the page people get, one rebuild path — web, infra — 2026-10-07

Two open items left in `docs/STATUS.md` by the SSG robustness work below.

**1. The prerender proxy dropped every query string.** Since the first SSG commit (#11, 2026-01) the
static server in `ssgRender.mjs` cut `req.url` at `?` and proxied only the path. The cut was there to
find a static file on disk, not a cache key or a filter, and it was applied to the API and `/storage`
requests too. So `/api/authors?language=en&limit=12` reached the API as `/authors`, and every SSG page
was rendered with the API's defaults (books: 20, authors: 50) instead of what the SPA asks for. The
path still picks the handler and the file; the query now goes on to the API unchanged. Test first:
`proxy_QueryString_ReachesApiIntact` failed on the old code (`/en/authors` arrived without its query).

What crawlers get, measured by rendering all 185 local routes with the old and the new proxy against
the local API (a third run of the old code changed 0 pages, so every difference below is the fix):

| Page | Old (bots) | New (bots = people) |
|------|-----------|---------------------|
| `/en/authors` | 50 authors, "Page 1 of 6", ItemList of 50 | 12 authors, "Page 1 of 6", ItemList of 12 |
| `/en/books` | 20 books, "Page 1 of 8" | 12 books, "Page 1 of 8" |
| `/en/` | Popular shelf of 20 | Popular shelf of 12 |
| each `/en/authors/<slug>` | "Other authors" = the first 12 by name (A–E), the same on every page | the 12 most recent, as the SPA shows |

64 of 184 saved pages changed: those 3 lists and 61 author pages. No book or genre page changed. Same
order where the lists overlap (the books default sort *is* Popular). Production serves the old shape
today: Googlebot's `/en/authors/` has `numberOfItems: 50`, `/en/books/` 20.

The old pages were inconsistent, not just different: "Page 1 of 6" over 50 items, and a JSON-LD
ItemList that did not match what a person sees. The cost of the fix is internal links: `/en/authors`
links 12 authors instead of 50, and the author pages link a different 12. Every author is still
linked from its books and from the sitemap. Pages 2+ are noindex and not prerendered, as before.

Rebuild cost: the same 889 API calls for 185 routes (a query changes parameters, not the number of
calls), 22 % fewer response bytes (1.79 → 1.40 MB), wall time 82 s → 81 s (a repeat of the old run took
89 s). No page grew by more than 1 % (author pages, ~120 bytes of other names); `/en/authors` shrank
from 38 KB to 16 KB.

**2. One rebuild path.** `make rebuild-ssg` ran `prerender.mjs` on the host and swapped `ssg-new` in
shell, with none of the worker's guards: no survival floor, no carry-forward of failed routes' pages,
no emptying of a leftover `ssg-new`, no stall detection, no Sentry, no job row (so `/health/ready` and
deploy's SSG wait could not see it). It now runs `infra/scripts/rebuild-ssg.sh`: the same
`POST /internal/ssg/rebuild-all` the nightly backup makes, then it follows the job row (via
`docker compose exec db`) and exits 0 only on `Completed`. Ctrl-C stops following, not the job.

Code review added three things. **It gives up** instead of polling forever: exit 1 if the worker has
not written a count in 5 min (`SSG_START_TIMEOUT_SECS`; the row has no "picked up" field — the API
marks a queued job `Running` and fills `total_routes` itself — and the worker writes counts after
every batch), or if the counts stop moving for 30 min (`SSG_STALL_TIMEOUT_SECS`; generous because the
retry pass writes its counts only when it ends, and the worker already stops its own stalled jobs at
5 min). The message says to inspect and restart ssg-worker, and the incident runbook now does the same
before `make rebuild-ssg`: the old host-side render used to work around a dead worker, and nothing does
now. **It does not report someone else's job as its own:** when a Full rebuild is already queued or
running, the API skips the new one; that job may have read its routes before this call, so the script
waits for it to end and then queues its own. **The proxy matches whole segments:** `/api`, `/api/…`,
`/storage`, `/storage/…` only; `/apiary` used to be proxied. The shell swap is deleted; nothing else used it (`prerender.mjs`'s flags are the worker's, and
`clean-ssg` still clears the worker's `ssg-old`). The docs no longer suggest running `prerender.mjs` by
hand on the server.

Smoke test on a scratch compose project (`-p ssgfollow`, own volume and port; the owner's stack
untouched), with the branch's ssg-worker image and a planted page in the live tree:

| Scenario | Result |
|---|---|
| `make rebuild-ssg` | job queued, progress printed, `Completed: 24 rendered, 0 failed of 24`, exit 0; planted page gone (promoted), no `ssg-new` left |
| second call while a Full job renders | `skipped` → waited for that job, then queued and followed its own; 2 jobs, both exit 0 |
| worker stopped | exit 1 after the start timeout (15 s in the test), "has not started … restart ssg-worker"; job left queued |
| second call, queued job, worker started 3 s later | waited for the queued job (24/24), then its own (24/24), exit 0 |
| worker killed mid-render | exit 1 after the stall timeout (15 s), "has not moved … probably dead" |
| API stopped mid-job | `Failed: 8 rendered, 16 failed of 24` with the worker's refusal text, exit non-zero; live tree checksum unchanged |

`scripts/scan-image-secrets.sh` on the rebuilt ssg-worker image (canary `.env`, as `images.yml`):
clean, 9 layers.

<a id="2026-10-07-ssg-worker-robustness"></a>

## SSG worker: hung rebuilds end, broken renders are not promoted, DB restarts survived — web, infra — 2026-10-07

Four bugs found by the smoke tests of #756. Each has a test that failed on the old code
(`apps/web/scripts/ssgJob.test.mjs`, `ssgRender.test.mjs`). To make them testable, prerender's
server and render and the worker's build checks moved into `ssgRender.mjs` and `ssgJob.mjs`. That was
a separate commit with no behaviour change; its first real run caught a dropped `emitEvent`.

**1. A hung API kept a rebuild `Running` forever.** prerender's proxy opened one request to the API
for each API call a page made. It had no timeout, and nothing closed it when the page gave up. Against
an API that accepts connections and never answers, those requests outlived their pages and kept
prerender's event loop alive after its last route. Reproduced on the host: the renders finished, the
summary printed, and the process was still alive at 240 s. The worker waited on it with no limit, so
the job stayed `Running`. That also delayed deploys: deploy.yml's "Wait for any running SSG rebuild"
counts a `Running` job started in the last 60 min as active, and waits up to 40 min before it deploys
anyway. Fix:

- the upstream request now closes when the page's request closes;
- a 30 s upstream timeout answers 504;
- `stopServer` closes every open connection instead of waiting for them;
- a 10 s `unref`'d exit backstop runs after a finished run.

On the worker side, prerender is stopped when it stops making progress. The first version used a
fixed 35-min deadline, which code review replaced with stall detection (below). SIGINT goes first:
Chrome runs in its own process group, and puppeteer kills that group and exits on SIGINT. SIGKILL
follows after 10 s. The job ends `Failed` with the reason, Sentry gets it with the job id (as in
#756), and `dist/ssg` is left alone. The worker's `/ssg/routes` and IndexNow fetches also get a 30 s
timeout.

**2. An empty build of one route was promoted.** The survival floor was `floor(n × 0.9)`, which is 0
for n = 1. It is now `max(1, floor(0.9n))` for n ≥ 1, and 0 only for n = 0. The test checks every n
from 1 to 5000: at least 1, at most n, and never below the old floor. Only non-empty regular
`index.html` files count. `ssg-new` is emptied when a job starts, because a leftover from a killed
job would otherwise count towards the floor.

**3. A page rendered against a broken API was saved as SSG.** The render waits for content *or an
error state*, and rejected only a timeout or a skeleton. BookDetailPage's error state has an `<h1>`
and no noindex, so it was saved as the page. Three signals were possible:

- the app's error state: the existing `[class*="error"]` / "not found" / "404" text match is already
  broad, and could not be made stricter without risk on good pages;
- a ready marker set by each page after its data loads: this would touch every SEO page;
- **the network: chosen.** A render fails when any `/api/` call's *last* outcome is a 5xx, a 429 or
  no response. It is the last outcome because the app retries, and a 500 followed by a 200 means the
  data arrived.

This check runs before the noindex check, because the error states for a missing book or author also
carry noindex. A noindex skip drops the page from the tree; a failure keeps the old one. For each
failed route, the worker copies the live page into `ssg-new` before the swap
(`carryForwardFailedPages`). It does not do this for a noindex skip, and never copies over a page
rendered this time. A failure writes no file, so failures count against the survival floor: when
more than 10 % fail, the whole build is refused. The atomic swap is unchanged.

False-positive check: all 185 routes of the owner's local catalog (read-only), old code against new.
Both produced the same 184 pages (one legitimate noindex) in the same 90 s.

**4. A database restart crashed the worker.** When an idle client's connection dies, `pg.Pool`
emits `'error'`. With no listener, the EventEmitter throws, the worker crashes, and compose restarts
it, possibly mid-job. Now a listener logs the error and reports it through the same once-per-outage
reporter as the poll loop. pg has already dropped that client and reconnects on the next query.

**Smoke test.** A scratch compose project `ssgsmoke` (the owner's stack untouched): the branch's
ssg-worker image, the owner's api/migrator images, a fault-injecting proxy between the worker and the
API, a fake Sentry ingest, and a deadline of 90 s. It used 24 routes (the seeded catalog).

| Scenario | Result |
|---|---|
| normal rebuild | 24/24 rendered and promoted, 12 s |
| API stopped after 3 routes | `Failed` at 92 s, "still running at the job deadline"; live tree checksum unchanged; 0 Chromium left (first version, fixed deadline) |
| API hangs (accepts, never answers) | `Failed` at 90 s, same reason; tree unchanged; 0 Chromium; no `ssg-new` left (first version, fixed deadline) |
| 500 on one author's API | 23 rendered, that page kept from the live tree (marker survived), the rest re-rendered, promoted |
| 500 on `/authors` (list) | 9 pages failed, below the floor of 21 → refused, live tree kept. The author detail pages failed too: their "other authors" section calls `/authors`, and the app hides that section when the call fails, so before this change they were saved without those links |
| DB restart, worker idle | worker not restarted (RestartCount 0), one log line, one Sentry event; next job completed |
| DB restart mid-job | worker survived; one route hit the API's own DB blip, rendered on retry; 24/24 promoted |

Sentry (fake ingest): each `Failed` job had one event with its job id, including both deadline stops.
Each DB restart gave one event. Each failed route gave one event naming the failing API path.
`scripts/scan-image-secrets.sh` on the rebuilt image (built with a canary `.env`, as `images.yml`
does): clean, 9 layers.

**Code review fixes, before merge.** Each has a test that fails without it.

- **(medium) A real 404 counted as an API failure.** The app throws on a 404 without reading the
  body, and the browser then aborts that body: a `requestfailed` for the same URL, after the
  response. Outcomes were keyed by URL, last one winning, so the 404 became "no response". A deleted
  or renamed book's stale page would then have been carried forward forever. The same thing happens
  with a 503, a retried 200, and then a late abort of the first request. Outcomes are now keyed by
  puppeteer's request object: a request that got a status keeps it, and only a request that never got
  one is "no response". The URL's outcome is that of its last request that has one, so retries still
  work. Tests cover 404 + abort (noindex skip), 503 → 200 → late abort (saved), and an unanswered
  request (failure). The reviewer reproduced it, and so did the test.
- **(low) The retry kept a first failure over a later noindex.** A route that failed once (a
  transient 503) and rendered noindex on retry (now a draft, or gone) kept the failure, and got its old
  page carried forward. A retry now replaces the earlier result when it is definitive, rendered or
  noindex, and a noindex result is not retried at all. `processRoutes` moved into `ssgRender.mjs` with
  an injectable renderer, so the loop is tested without a browser.
- **(low) The fixed 35-min deadline would have thrown away good builds**: against a partly slow API,
  or once the library grows ~40 %, all of them, visible only in Sentry. It is replaced by **stall
  detection**: the job is stopped when no route has rendered for 5 min (`SSG_JOB_STALL_MS`), plus a
  cap of max(60 min, 2 s per route) (`SSG_JOB_DEADLINE_MS`). Progress comes from the per-route
  `result` lines prerender already prints, which the worker already reads, so no new channel was
  needed. One deviation from "any completed route is progress": in the first pass a *failure* does
  not count. A hung API still completes every route, as a failure at the 30 s navigation timeout, so
  counting failures would have kept a hung job alive until the cap. In the retry pass every attempt
  counts, since that pass is all failures and a few persistently broken routes must not stall a
  good build. The cost: ~5 min of consecutive first-pass failures (~160 routes in a row) stops a build
  that might have passed the floor.

Re-checked after the fixes:

- all 185 local catalog routes plus `/en/books/does-not-exist` and `/en/authors/does-not-exist`, main
  against new: the same 184 pages, and both not-found routes are noindex skips (dropped, not carried);
- smoke with a 60 s stall window: a normal rebuild still promotes (24/24). A hung API (from the
  second batch) ends `Failed` 64 s into the job, about 60 s after its last rendered route, with
  "rendered no route for 1.0 min" and one Sentry event; the live tree is untouched and no Chromium is
  left;
- the image re-scanned with a canary `.env`: clean.

**Found, not fixed (in STATUS):**

- the proxy drops the query string of every API call, so SSG pages are rendered with the API's
  default `limit`/`sort`;
- `make rebuild-ssg` swaps in shell, with neither the floor nor the carry-forward;
- the rule is strict by design: an endpoint that is broken on every page blocks every rebuild until
  it is fixed. That shows up as `Failed` jobs and Sentry events, not as degraded pages.

<a id="2026-10-07-observability-ssg-mcp"></a>

## Observability: Sentry on mcp-server and ssg-worker, OTLP off in production — backend, infra — 2026-10-07

Architecture review item #22 ([summary](../01-architecture/review-2026-10/00-summary.md), source
[ops §5](../01-architecture/review-2026-10/04-ops-reliability.md)): "No metrics in prod (OTLP points at
a container that isn't started); no Sentry on ssg-worker / mcp-server." Where things now stand:
[delivery.md § Observability](../01-architecture/delivery.md#observability).

**What was there.** Sentry on the API (ASP.NET pipeline, 20 % of request traces, every agent run) and
the Worker (logging provider, errors only), both through `SentryScrubber`'s allowlist; mobile in its
own project with its own scrubber. Nothing on mcp-server — whose tool catalog turned every unexpected
exception into a clean `"… failed: unexpected error"` for the model and logged **nothing**, so a broken
mapping on a public auth surface looked exactly like a quiet day — and nothing on the ssg-worker, whose
failures reached only the job row and the container log. OpenTelemetry pointed both .NET services at
`http://aspire-dashboard:18889`; the dashboard sits behind the `observability` profile, which the deploy
never starts. Every traces/logs/metrics batch was built, failed DNS and was dropped, without one log
line (the exporter reports through an EventSource) — confirmed on a local stack: `getent hosts
aspire-dashboard` → nothing, zero exporter lines in 14 h of API logs.

**OpenTelemetry.** Registered only when `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` is set; compose now passes
`${OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT:-}` instead of the hard-coded Aspire address. The console exporter that
stood in for a missing endpoint is deleted (with its package): in a Development container it would have
printed every span and metric into the log — which the owner's local stack would have started doing
the moment the endpoint went away. Aspire remains the local tool (profile + endpoint in `.env`). A
hosted metrics backend is one env var and an auth header away, and deliberately not chosen here: cost,
and OTLP spans carry full SQL and the client IP, which never pass the Sentry scrubber.

**Shared .NET setup.** `SentryBootstrap`, `SentryScrubber`, the logging registration and
`TraceRedactor` moved from Infrastructure/Ai.Llm into `backend/src/Observability/TextStack.Observability`
(Sentry and nothing else) so the MCP server — which must not reference Application/Infrastructure —
uses the identical scrubber. Every host now sets a `service` tag (`api`, `worker`, `mcp-server`;
ssg-worker sets `ssg-worker`), on the allowlist, so one project serves all four.

**Scrubbing, all hosts.** Free text (messages, exception values, breadcrumbs) now also loses `Bearer`
values, `tsk_` connect keys, `tso_` OAuth tokens, JWTs, the `/mcp/k/<key>` segment and any `?key=value`
query; an error event's `request.url` loses its query too (before, only transactions did).

**mcp-server.** Sentry through the logging provider, in **http mode only** — the stdio tool runs on a
reader's machine, where a `SENTRY_DSN` belongs to someone else. The catalog's catch-all logs an Error
naming the tool and nothing else. On top of the shared scrubber: exception messages replaced (a
`FormatException` quotes its input, and here every input is the reader's text — type and stack kept),
and Information lines are not breadcrumbs. The logging provider attaches no request, so the
`Authorization` header and the connect-URL path never enter an event; the hosting scope's `RequestPath`
tag dies on the allowlist. Verified end to end against a fake ingest: a `tools/call` through
`/mcp/k/<key>` with reader text in the arguments produced one event — tag `service`, exception type,
`[redacted]` value, no breadcrumbs, no request; neither the key nor the text anywhere in the envelope.

**ssg-worker.** `@sentry/node` via the catalog, pinned to **10.75.2** — the `@sentry/core` that
mobile's `@sentry/react-native` 8.28 pins, so the hoisted workspace keeps one core (a caret took 10.76.1
and nested four extra copies). Not 11.x: it makes `@sentry/bundler-plugins` a hard dependency, which
drags the 15 MB `sentry` CLI and a native parser into a runtime image. The SDK is imported only when
`SENTRY_DSN` is set; `defaultIntegrations: false` (no console/HTTP breadcrumbs, no request data, no local
variables), uncaught-exception and **strict** unhandled-rejection handlers (Sentry's default `warn` would
have turned a crash into a worker that carries on), no tracing, no OpenTelemetry setup. Reported: a job
that ends Failed (tag `ssg.job_id`), each route still failing after prerender's retries (tags
`ssg.route`, `ssg.route_type`; the first line of the error only — prerender appends the page's console
and HTML below it; noindex skips excluded; capped at 20 per job, one fingerprint), a poll-loop failure
once until the loop recovers, and a fatal crash. Same environment rules as .NET (`Development` reports
nothing; `Production` without a release is `production-unverified`); `SENTRY_RELEASE` from a new
`GIT_SHA` build arg in both images.

Caught by the docker smoke test, fixed before merge: tags set with `withScope` leaked — without
OpenTelemetry's context manager the scope did not isolate, and the next unrelated event (a pg
"terminating connection" crash) arrived tagged with the previous job. Tags now travel per event
(capture context), with a test that fails on the old code.

**Code-review fixes, before merge.** (1) The token regex knew `tsk_`/`tso_` but not `tsr_` — the
OAuth refresh token, our longest-lived credential — nor `tsc_`; it now covers all four, and both test
suites derive the list from the code that issues them (reflection over Application in .NET; the Node
test reads `OAuth.cs`/`McpKeys.cs`). (2) The SDK's DiagnosticSource integration records EF/Npgsql
`db.*` spans with the full SQL, which `ScrubTransaction` never touched — it is now disabled for every
host (API traces lose their database child spans, nothing else), and any `db.*` span description is
blanked as a backstop. (3) Sentry's HTTP handler on outgoing calls: `CaptureFailedRequests` off and
`TracePropagationTargets` empty for every host; on mcp-server the handler is not attached at all. Each
fix has a test that fails without it.

**Images** (compressed, then on disk): ssg-worker 410.7 → 417.5 MB (+6.9 MB), 1.49 → 1.56 GB (the
`node_modules` layer 160 → 221 MB); mcp-server 52.9 → 53.2 MB. Both scanned clean with a canary `.env`.
The MCP NuGet tool gains `Sentry.dll` + `Sentry.Extensions.Logging.dll` (~0.9 MB), never engaged in stdio.

**Smoke test** (scratch compose project, owner's stack untouched): ssg-worker rendered 24/24 routes of an
empty catalog with Sentry on; a job with the API down → one event tagged with its job id; a route that
times out → one event with its path and "Navigation timeout of 30000 ms exceeded", no HTML; mcp-server
`/health` ok with a fake DSN; a broken tool mapping → one scrubbed event.

**Owner step.** Nothing new if the server `.env` already has `SENTRY_DSN` — api/worker need it to
report at all; check with `grep -c '^SENTRY_DSN=.' .env` on the server. mcp-server and ssg-worker read
the same variable (compose also passes `ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT` to mcp-server now, so a local
`Development` `.env` keeps it quiet like the others). Remove any `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` there unless
something receives it. In Sentry, filter by `service:mcp-server` / `service:ssg-worker`.

<a id="2026-10-07-migrations-owned-by-the-migrator"></a>

## Migrations owned by the migrator (review #17) — backend, infra, docs — 2026-10-07

Review item #17 (backend N6): migrations ran twice, in the `migrator` container and in
`db.Database.Migrate()` at Api start, so an Api restart undid a `MIGRATE_TARGET` rollback. Decision:
[ADR-021](../01-architecture/adr/ADR-021-migrations-owned-by-the-migrator.md).

**Already shipped in #706 (2026-10-05):** the Api migrates only with `ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT=Development`
and `Database:MigrateOnStartup=true`, which only `launchSettings.json` sets (`dotnet run`).

**This change**

- The decision moved to `Api/Extensions/MigrationPolicy.cs`, with unit tests: Development + flag is
  the only yes; Production/Staging/Test with the flag, and Development without it, are no.
- **Schema behind the build is visible, not fatal.** At start the Api logs Critical (Sentry) with the
  pending count and newest name. `/health/ready` gained `components.schema` — `ok`, or
  `behind` + `pending[]` with 503 — re-checked per request, so it clears the moment the migrator runs.
  Not a start-up throw: with `restart: always` that is a crash-loop through a deliberate rollback.
  `/health` (liveness, compose healthcheck, deploy gate) is unchanged. New integration test
  `SchemaReadinessTests` asserts `schema: ok` on the CI stack, i.e. that the migrator brought it up.
- **Rollback order, and a migrator that refuses the wrong one** (code review). The first draft said
  "roll the code back first" — wrong: `rollback_commit` re-tags `textstack-migrator:latest` to the old
  image, which has no `Down()` for the newer migrations, so a targeted run after it reverted nothing
  and exited 0. Order now: schema first with the current image, check `/health/ready` shows the
  expected `pending`, then `rollback_commit` (whose no-target migrator run is a no-op on the
  rolled-back schema). `migrate.sh` reads `__EFMigrationsHistory` (psql added to the migrator image)
  and exits 1 when a target is set and the DB holds migrations the image does not contain; without a
  target it notes them and continues. Also: the Critical start-up log no longer says "run the
  migrator" unconditionally (that would undo a deliberate rollback), and `/health/ready` reports
  `schema: skipped` with the db down instead of paying a second connect timeout.
- **`health-check.yml` alarms under its own label.** "Readiness probe" fetches `/api/health/ready` once
  (no `-f`, so a 503 keeps its body) and fails only on no response, non-JSON or db/storage down;
  "Schema matches the build" fails with `::error::` naming the pending migrations; "SSG freshness"
  reuses the same response. Before, a 503 would have failed the SSG step's `curl -sf`. actionlint
  1.7.12 (pinned digest): same 10 SC2086 infos as main (the deliberately unquoted `$CURL_RETRY`).
- **The rollback never worked.** Verifying it found that #12 (2026-01-23) dropped `MIGRATE_TARGET`
  from the migrator's compose `environment:`. `MIGRATE_TARGET=0 docker compose up migrator` logged
  `Target: latest` and did nothing. It is now `docker compose run --rm -e MIGRATE_TARGET=<name>
  migrator` — deliberately not restored as `${MIGRATE_TARGET:-}`, because compose reads `.env` on
  every `up` and a target left there would roll production back on every deploy.

**Verified on a scratch compose project** (`-p`, named volumes, own port; the running stack untouched,
then `down -v`): fresh volume → migrator applied all 144, Api healthy, `schema: ok`; old rollback form
→ `Target: latest`, no-op; `run -e` to the previous migration → reverted one; `restart api` and
`up -d --force-recreate --no-deps api` → stayed rolled back, 0 restarts, `/health` 200, `/health/ready`
503 `{"schema":{"status":"behind","pending":["20261005185848_ChapterDependentsNoActionOnDelete"]}}`,
Critical line in the log; Development without the flag (CI's shape) → no migration; Development +
flag → migrated forward; plain `up -d api` → the migrator re-ran to head and `/health/ready` went back
to 200 without an Api restart. `SchemaReadinessTests` passed at head and failed (`behind`) when rolled
back. Migrator guard, second scratch run: a planted unknown row (`29990101000000_FromTheFuture`)
→ targeted run exits 1, nothing reverted; no target → note, exit 0; row removed → targeted rollback
works; fresh volume (no history table) → success; `MIGRATE_TARGET=NoSuchMigration` → EF "not found",
exit 1. Db stopped → `/health/ready` 503 in 67 ms with `schema: skipped`.
<a id="2026-10-06-delivery-hardening"></a>

## Delivery hardening: digests, pins, scans, faster skips (#746) — infra — 2026-10-06

Follow-up to #742 (build once on GitHub), from the delivery review the same day. Goal: ship faster,
security first, zero secret leaks. Pipeline doc: [`delivery.md`](../01-architecture/delivery.md);
decision: [ADR-020](../01-architecture/adr/ADR-020-build-once-deploy-by-digest.md).

**Security**

- **Deploy pulls by digest.** `images` records the digest `docker push` reports for each image (the
  bytes it scanned) and outputs `<svc>@sha256:…`; the server pulls `name@digest` and tags it `:<sha>`
  locally. A `:sha` re-pushed in GHCR between build and pull no longer reaches production. No digest →
  server build, as before.
- **Actions pinned by commit SHA** with a `# vX.Y` comment — all of them, first-party included
  (`pnpm/action-setup`, `expo/expo-github-action`, `peter-evans/create-pull-request`, `NuGet/login`
  were the ones next to secrets).
- **Pulled images pinned by digest:** `pgvector` (prod DB), `ollama`, `aspire-dashboard` (was
  `:latest`, now `13.6.0`), restic in `backup.yml` (sees the R2 keys and the prod `.env`), the alpine
  that `make fix-permissions` runs as root over `./data` on every deploy, and the scanner. The scanner
  moved from `alpine` + `apk add grep` to `debian:13-slim` (GNU grep built in), so a scan needs no
  package mirror.
- **Dependabot `docker` and `docker-compose` lanes**, weekly, grouped; pgvector majors ignored (a
  major is dump/restore).
- **`rollback_commit`** goes through a `guard` job: 7–40 lowercase hex, resolves in a full clone of
  main, ancestor of `origin/main`. Everything downstream gets the full SHA via `env:`; the server
  re-checks ancestry against its own clone.
- **Least privilege:** every workflow has a top-level `permissions:` — `contents: read`, or `{}` for
  backup and health-check. `deps-refresh` moved its write scopes to its one job.
- **Canaries from compose:** the canary `.env` now also takes every `${VAR}` in the compose files —
  the hand list had already missed `MOBILE_MIN_SUPPORTED_BUILD`.
- **The two artifacts that reach every user are scanned:** `apps/web/dist` after the server build
  (patterns + the server's real `*SECRET/*PASSWORD/*TOKEN/*API_KEY` values; prints the file, never the
  value), and the OTA bundle before `eas update` (an `expo export` under the EAS production
  environment, plus `EXPO_TOKEN`'s value). `scripts/scan-image-secrets.sh --dir` is the new mode.
  Stray `apps/web/.env*` files on the server are moved aside before vite runs — the four `VITE_`
  variables the code reads are passed inline anyway.

**Speed**

- `deploy.yml` skips pushes that only touch `apps/mobile/**`, `extension/**` or the mobile
  workflows (the #745 merge cost 21 min for no server change). Never `packages/**` or the lockfile.
- The pre-deploy `pg_dump` stays on the critical path, now after the SSG wait and before the web
  build — a failed dump ships nothing, and the migrator follows minutes later. A first version moved it to a parallel job to save ~2.5 min;
  review caught that the dump could then be an hour older than the migration it exists to undo. The
  rollback point won.
- Review fixes before merge: the deploy runs the scanner from the workflow's own commit (a rollback
  target may predate it), the dist scan covers only vite's output (`index.html` + `assets/`, not the
  SSG trees ssg-worker rewrites mid-scan), and the OTA scan runs before the store build as well.
- *Free disk space* stays in `images.yml` until the slim Dockerfiles land and a run shows the room.

**Verified locally:** actionlint (no new findings vs main), `docker compose config`, every pinned
digest resolves, scan script against built images (clean) and a planted image (canary in a deleted
file + `id_rsa` both caught), against a fresh `apps/web/dist` (clean) and planted dirs (token, value,
`.env` caught; empty dir fails), `docker push` digest parsing against a local registry.

<a id="2026-10-06-privacy-policy-matches-code"></a>

## Privacy policy matches the code; nginx stops logging query strings — legal, infra — 2026-10-06

**Why.** Before the Play production application (~2026-10-16) the Data Safety form and the policy
were re-derived from the code. Both were wrong. The worst line: "if you do not use the tutor,
Explain or translation, nothing is sent to OpenAI". Every upload goes to the enrichment agent
(`Ai:Routes` `bookmeta.agent` → `openai-explain`, `UserBookEnrichmentService.ApplyEnrichmentAsync`):
title, author and the first 600 characters of chapter one (`EnrichmentAgent`). The agent also asks
Open Library about the title and author. The policy also said that reading without an account keeps
data on the device. That is false on mobile, where `SessionGate` mints a server-side guest when a
book opens. And the runbook's Data Safety table still said "no crash SDK on mobile", a month after
mobile Sentry was armed.

**What.**
- **Policy** (`packages/shared/src/i18n/en.json` → `privacy.*`, one copy rendered by web and mobile
  through `PRIVACY_SECTIONS`). New paragraphs: `collectBody7` (mobile Sentry + Expo update check),
  `thirdPartiesOpenLibrary`, `thirdPartiesExpo`, `thirdPartiesConnectors` (MCP/OAuth assistants
  are the reader's own choice and fall under the assistant's policy), `retentionBody4` (nightly
  backups: two on the server, restic to R2 encrypted, `--keep-monthly 3` ≈ 90 days). Rewritten:
  guest accounts and their cleanup rule (30 days inactive *and* nothing saved; `GuestCleanupWorker`),
  the models (translate `gpt-4.1-nano`; Explain, tutor and enrichment `gpt-4.1-mini`), AI request
  records (`llm_traces.user_id` is always null and emails/phones are redacted, but the passage and
  question are kept; tutor and enrichment are traced at 100%, translate/explain at 10%; tutor
  sessions and `agent_run` *are* account-linked until deletion), Microsoft TTS (consumer Edge
  endpoint, no agreement), caches (translate/explain/TTS 30 days), request logs 14 days. Dated
  6 October 2026.
- **Delete page** (`apps/web/src/locales/en.json` → `deleteAccount.retentionBody`): "nothing in
  them identifies you afterwards" overstated it, because traces keep the reader's question verbatim.
  The page now lists what outlives the delete and for how long.
- **nginx** (`infra/nginx/textstack.conf`): `log_format textstack_noquery`. It is `combined` with the
  request line rebuilt from `$request_uri` minus `?…`, and the Referer stripped the same way. It is
  set as `access_log` in all three server blocks. Server-level, because an http-level `access_log`
  would add a second log next to `nginx.conf`'s instead of replacing it. `$request_uri` and not
  `$uri`, because `try_files` rewrites `$uri` to `/index.html` on every SPA route. Before this,
  `/api/tts?text=<passage>` and `?q=<search>` sat next to the client IP for the logrotate window.
- **Runbook** (`docs/03-ops/play-store-release.md`): the Data Safety section is the full answer
  set, with the reason for each non-obvious choice.

**Trade-offs.** We declare more than strictly needed. Files & docs and Other UGC are *Shared: Yes*
because Microsoft and Open Library are not our service providers. OpenAI is disclosed plainly and
not leaned on as a processor under a DPA, so the form stays true whatever the contract state is.
The cheaper fix for the upload leak was to route `bookmeta.agent` to Ollama. The owner kept OpenAI
and chose to disclose it.

**Container logs.** `docker-compose.yml` had no log rotation, so the application logs (API, Worker,
everything else on json-file) had no bound at all, and "logs 14 days" was not true of them. A shared
`x-logging` anchor (`json-file`, `max-size: 10m`, `max-file: 5`) now applies to every service. That
bound is by size, not time, so the policy says so: overwritten "typically within days to a few
weeks". It takes effect when a container is recreated, which `deploy.yml` does when the config changes.

**Known limit.** nginx's *error* log still writes the full request line, query string included, for
a request it rejects (a `limit_req` 503 on `/api/tts?text=…`, for example). Not changed:
`/etc/logrotate.d/nginx` rotates `/var/log/nginx/*.log` with `rotate 14`, so the error log is held
14 days like the access log, and the policy's 14-day statement covers it.
The policy says so in as many words (`collectBody5`: the error log can hold a rejected or failed
request in full), rather than claiming read-aloud text and searches never reach a log.

**Sentry transactions (review of #738).** `beforeSend` scrubbing never saw performance data:
transactions go through `beforeSendTransaction`, which neither client set. On mobile, the SDK's
fetch spans record the full URL (description, `url`, `http.query`), so in the 10% of sampled
sessions a search or read-aloud request went to Sentry with its text. `scrubTransaction`
(`apps/mobile/src/lib/sentryScrub.ts`) now cuts the whole query string from spans, the trace
context and the request. On the backend, `SentryScrubber.ScrubTransaction` already filtered tags but
left `Request.QueryString` and outgoing span descriptions (an Open Library lookup carries an
upload's title). It now strips both. Web has no Sentry SDK.

<a id="2026-10-06-reader-bug-hunt-r3"></a>

## Reader bug hunt, round R3 — mobile, data/sync, web — 2026-10-06

**Why.** R3 is the rest of the 47 findings from the R1/R2 hunt (the Mediums and Lows), plus what R2
deferred. Same bar as before: one PR per track, tests that fail first, code review, a fix for what
review found. Background: [R1 + R2](#2026-10-05-reader-bug-hunt-r1-r2).

### Mobile (#723) — JS only, ships as OTA

- **Android Back closed the book** while the word toolbar was open (M3). Back now closes the toolbar
  first.
- **Signed out on a dead network, every chapter was blank for 3 s** (M4). Each `SessionGate` waited its
  full 3 s deadline again. Now, after one gate gives up, gates skip the wait for 5 minutes; a success
  clears that.
- **Uploads used the app language, not the book's** (M5), for TTS, translate, explain and the saved
  word (`lib/bookLanguage.ts`).
- **No recovery when the WebView renderer died** (M6). The WebView now remounts at the saved place,
  reflow and PDF.
- **PDF page input hid under the keyboard** (M7). It moved into a modal above the keyboard.
- **Session "words read" was inflated** (M8). Counting starts after the restore lands, and every
  programmatic jump (newer position, toast, foreground check, rebuild, reflow) goes through one
  `issueRestore` ref and is not counted.
- **Font rebuild restored by %** (L1), now by text anchor with % as fallback. **The last partial
  heartbeat tick was dropped** (L2), now counted once. **PDF 401 + failed refresh was silent** (L4),
  now a toast with Retry. **Clock trust** (L5): a tick is clamped to 0–60 s, and
  `serverProvablyNewer` caps the local stamp at now + 5 min.
- **Review found 2:** a gate's deadline could overwrite a fast success (the next chapter then waited
  again), and programmatic jumps were still counted as reading. Both fixed with tests (`f782a1b2`).

### Data/sync (#724) — no migration

- **Guest merge dropped an upload** whose slug the account already used, with its progress; its
  highlights were orphaned (M1). The upload is now renamed (`-<8 hex of its id>`) and moved with its
  progress and highlights. Nothing is dropped, so the dropped-file cleanup code was deleted. A
  SQLSTATE-23 conflict still returns `false`, never throws (ADR-014).
- **Highlight version check was not atomic** (L2). The update is one conditional `ExecuteUpdate`;
  0 rows → 409 with the current row, or 404.
- **Bad reader writes gave 500** (L4, web L5). Highlight colour, anchor shape and required fields, and
  vocabulary field lengths are validated → 400. `ExceptionMiddleware` maps `BadHttpRequestException`
  to its own status for every endpoint.
- **Streaks were judged in UTC** (L6). `POST /me/reading/sessions?tz=` sends the reader's offset;
  `ParseTzOffset` clamps it to ±14 h.
- **The ADR-018 limits are closed.** Admin chapter delete and quality-pipeline delete/merge now
  rewrite slug locators, positions and insights (`PlanFromIds`), and `MaxChapterNumber` is remapped on
  renumber.
- **Review:** the stricter validation was checked against real web, mobile and MCP payloads — nothing
  legitimate is rejected. One finding: uploads skipped the new stamp clamp (L1). Fixed, with a test
  that fails without it.

### Web (#725)

- **In-book search counts did not match the painted results** (M2). The drawer and the overlay used
  different matchers (NBSP, inline tags). Now one matcher, `findTextOffsets` in reader-overlay, over
  the chapter article only: 246 = 246 in the browser. The search index resets on chapter change (L3).
- **A removed pending vocab word was still saved** when a guest was minted (M3). It is now deleted
  from IndexedDB.
- **TTS kept speaking** after a chapter change or leaving (L1): `useTts(stopKey)`. **The "finished
  book" screen was unreachable** (L2): on the last chapter, Next is now "Finish book".
- **An offline bookmark delete came back** (L4). This is the offline bookmark queue R2 built and
  descoped: `lib/bookmarkStore.ts` + `lib/bookmarkSync.ts`, local-first pending rows and tombstones,
  one IndexedDB transaction per step, one sync at a time, rows per owner (`anon`, `guest:<id>`, user).
  Uploads use the same queue; `useUserBookBookmarks` was deleted. Old rows migrate as synced, on
  purpose.
- **Pending highlights with `editionId:slug` ids** (made on a cached chapter before #719) are repaired
  to real chapter ids before POST; one that cannot be resolved is never sent.
- **PDF `?highlight=` jump** runs once per link, after load, and wins over the open-page scroll.
- **Review found 5, all in the bookmark queue:** a tombstone could delete another device's bookmark;
  without IndexedDB the queue did nothing; guest rows were stranded after sign-in; a revived tombstone
  was dropped; one misleading comment. Each fixed with a failing test first (`fa57c28a`).

**Lessons.**
1. The longest R3 change was the one R2 descoped. Descoping was right: it needed five review fixes on
   its own.
2. "Make the 500 a 400" is only safe after replaying what real clients send. #724 did that before
   tightening validation.
3. Every mobile fix is still unit-tested only. The device checks are collected in one phone checklist
   for the owner.

<a id="2026-10-06-reader-r4-and-adr-019"></a>

## Reader R4 and ADR-019: shared rules, not one state machine — mobile, web, shared — 2026-10-06

**Why.** About two thirds of the 47 findings were restore/save/sync logic, written twice, once per
client. The first idea was one cross-platform restore/save state machine.

**Consilium (2026-10-05).** Four reviewers read the draft — simplicity critic, adversarial QA, web,
mobile:
- the simplicity critic rejected it: the helpers already exist; the value is web catching up;
- adversarial QA found blockers: it would bring back R1 Criticals (C1 doc reset when there is no book
  key; a write gate read at fire time breaks web C1 or mobile H1), its positional landing was too wide,
  and its transitions were not total (a rebuild can arrive during a restore);
- mobile: not behaviour-preserving — moving React state into a ref changes which report is saved,
  when the baseline is set and how stale acks behave;
- web: low gain, because web lands a restore in the same rAF as its `scrollTo`.

**Decision: lift and adopt** ([ADR-019](../01-architecture/adr/ADR-019-reader-position-rules.md),
#727). Pure helpers live in `packages/shared/src/reader/` (`newerPosition.ts`,
`progressPrecedence.ts`, `pdfPersistGate.ts`); each host keeps its timing, I/O and toasts. Eight named
rules, each tied to a bug and a test. The reviews also found live bugs, fixed in R4. Two questions are
left for the owner (end of the ADR).

### Mobile R4 (#728) — JS only, OTA

- **Custom typography reopened the book at the top and saved 0.** Settings load async, so the document
  rebuilt during the open restore — on every open with non-default typography. Now
  `duringRestorePlan` (`lib/rebuildRestore.ts`): restore in flight → keep the pending target; landed →
  snapshot; not sent yet → wait. Typography is injected only into a loaded document. ADR-019 rule 8.
- **A PDF with a known start page dropped the device page.** The first jump now always waits for the
  device read (AsyncStorage, never the network): `lib/pdfInitialJump.ts`.
- **The foreground newer-check was skipped after Android killed the renderer** in the background. It
  now waits until the restore lands (`gateOpened`).
- The restore ack carries a chapter-relative `scrollY`, so a tiny restore is still booked as a jump.
  This closed a `ponytail:` ceiling from R3.
- **Review found 3:** a reflow/rebuild ack reset the move baseline (silent move after a font change);
  a second rebuild before load restored the old target (an L1 regression); typography was lost after
  PDF → "Read as text". Fixed with tests (`a38c55c5`).

### Web R4 (#729)

- **PDF saved page 1 before the resume jump** (data loss). `PdfOriginalView` adopts the shared
  `pdfPersistGate`.
- **Progress GETs had no timeout**, so a hung GET meant no restore and no save. Reader GETs now use a
  3 s timeout, the auth wait is capped at 3 s, and a failure is not an answer (404 → none, any other
  failure → unknown). `useNewerPositionCheck` asks again (0/10/30 s, on online, on visible). A late
  newer position moves only a reader who has not moved; an explicit position (`?highlight=`, TOC, PDF
  jump) is never replaced.
- **`wordsRead` ≈ 0:** session start is set once, and only the reader's own scrolls count.
- **A stale tab overwrote another device:** re-check on visible.
- **Duplicate PDF progress GET:** one, the bounded one.
- The newer-position helpers moved to `packages/shared/src/reader/newerPosition.ts`; mobile
  re-exports them.

**Web R4 took three review rounds** (7 + 7 + 4 findings). Round 2 added an input-gated rule: a scroll
counts as the reader's only right after a wheel/touch/key/pointer event. It lost real reading:
scrollbar drags in Firefox and Safari, autoscroll and Ctrl+F jumps send no such event, so they were
never saved. Round 3 (`ecc11d32`) replaced it with mobile's proven rule: our own scrolls set a
baseline, and a move of more than 48 px from it (`REFLOW_MOVE_TOLERANCE_PX`) is the reader's. Net
−7 lines. One known ceiling is marked `ponytail:` in the code: a large layout shift above the reading
line (a late image) after an open the server has not answered still reads as the reader moving.

**Lessons.**
1. A design review that reads the old bugs is cheaper than a rewrite that repeats them. The state
   machine looked clean because it had not met C1 yet.
2. When one client already solved a problem on real devices, copy its rule. The "smarter" web rule was
   both longer and wrong.
3. The review's best output was not the ADR but the eight live bugs (five web, three mobile) it found
   while checking it.

<a id="2026-10-06-cross-app-anchors"></a>

## Cross-app highlight anchors: shared fixtures, phone cross-paragraph fix — mobile, web, shared — 2026-10-06

**Why.** A highlight is made on one client and painted on another: web, the phone's WebView, or an
assistant over MCP. Each side was tested only against itself. R2's ghost-highlight fix
([#719](#2026-10-05-reader-bug-hunt-r1-r2)) showed the shapes differ.

**What (#730).** Shared fixtures in `packages/shared/src/reader/__fixtures__/anchors/`: chapter HTML
plus one anchor file per producer. `web.json` comes from the real web creator, `mobile.json` from the
real bridge run in jsdom, `mcp.json` mirrors `SynthesizeAnchor` (`McpToolCatalog.cs`). Cases: chapter
start/end, inline tags, across paragraphs, NBSP, a repeated phrase, Cyrillic, Arabic, repeats at the
chapter edges. Both resolvers must resolve every producer's anchor to the same text; text unique to
chapter A never paints in B; PDF anchors never resolve as text.

**The fixtures found a real bug on their first run.** A phone highlight across two paragraphs landed
2 characters early, or not at all. `exact` came from `Selection.toString()`, which serializes like
`innerText` (a paragraph break becomes newlines); prefix and suffix came from `Range.toString()`, the
raw text nodes the resolvers read. Now one function, `getRangeAnchor` in `readerBridge.ts`, takes all
three from the Range. Also: the phone's prefix/suffix were cut from `document.body`, so they could
include template whitespace and the inline script. They are now cut from the chapter element
(`anchorScope`), with the body as fallback for the PDF viewer.

**Known limits** (pinned by tests, open in STATUS): an MCP anchor has no context, so a repeated quote
lands on its first occurrence; `mcp.json` is written by hand, so it can drift from the C#.

**Lesson.** Each client had passing tests for its own anchors. The bug lived between two of them, and
only a test that crosses the boundary could see it.

<a id="2026-10-06-ci-speedup"></a>

## CI speed-up: path filters, Docker layer cache, PR concurrency — infra — 2026-10-06

**Why.** Every PR ran everything: a docs fix waited for Docker builds and Playwright.

**What (#726).**
- **Path filters.** A `changes` job (plain `git diff`, no third-party action) decides what runs.
  Docs-only skips backend, frontend, docker, e2e, mobile, extension; web-only skips backend and docker;
  mobile-only runs mobile and frontend. Shared paths (`packages/`, `scripts/`, `.github/`, root
  lockfiles, anything unknown) run everything. It fails open, and gated jobs start with `!cancelled()`
  so a broken filter cannot pass a required check green.
- **Docker layer cache.** buildx with `type=gha`, one scope per image, through
  `.github/docker-compose.ci.yml`, shared by `docker` and `e2e`. `ignore-error=true`: the cache can
  never fail CI.
- **Cache warming on main.** `ci-cache-warm.yml` builds the images after each code merge, so a PR's
  first run starts warm.
- **Concurrency.** A new push to a PR cancels its older run. Deploy calls and `deps/**` are never
  cancelled.
- **No deploy for docs-only.** `deploy.yml` ignores `docs/**` and root `*.md`.

**Cost.** The first run, cold cache, took 21 minutes. A mobile-only PR now finishes in about 2.5 (#731).

**Side effect.** Fast and slow PRs now finish at very different times. The same night, the mobile-only
PR finished first and merged; the PRs still in CI then had `CHANGELOG.md` conflicts. With parallel PRs
that each add a changelog line, expect a rebase after every merge.

<a id="2026-10-06-reader-shell-split"></a>

## Mobile `ReaderShell.tsx` split by job (1689 → 617 lines) — mobile — 2026-10-06

**Why.** After R3/R4, `ReaderShell.tsx` mixed restore, PDF, word actions, navigation, session feed and
the footer in one file. Every R-round fix touched it. The owner approved a split after R4.

**What (#731).** Code moved by line range, no rewrites: `useReaderPdf`, `useReaderDocument`,
`useReaderWordActions`, `useReaderChapterNav`, `useReaderSessionFeed`, `useReaderMessages`,
`ReaderFooter`, `ReaderExitPrompt`, `readerShellTypes` (all in `apps/mobile/src/components/reader/`).
R4's effect order (source reset → typography → chrome) is kept. `matchesChapter` moved to
`src/lib/highlightChapter.ts` with tests; before, it sat outside mobile vitest.

**How "no behaviour change" was checked.** No behaviour test was modified. Source-text wiring guards
were only re-pointed to the moved code, and 16 of 17 were mutation-checked (break the code, see the
guard fail). The 17th (M5 `textLanguage`) turned out weak on main and is now strict. Review compared
old and new hunk by hunk.

**One intended change:** the reflow WebView's `renderHighlight` now refuses a `kind:"pdf"` anchor —
found by the [cross-app fixtures](#2026-10-06-cross-app-anchors).

**Still open:** the same split for web `ReaderPage.tsx` / `ReaderHighlights.tsx`, and tests for the
mobile hooks themselves (STATUS).

<a id="2026-10-05-reader-bug-hunt-r1-r2"></a>

## Reader bug hunt, rounds R1 + R2 — backend, web, mobile — 2026-10-05

**Why.** Reading is the product, and the reader had collected small "it lost my place" reports. Instead
of fixing them one at a time, three agents hunted in parallel against one commit (`d92e83aa`): web
reader, mobile reader, data/sync. Every finding was ranked Critical / High / Medium / Low with
file:line evidence (47 in total), and the lead re-verified each Critical before any fix. R1 = the
Criticals (#713–#716), R2 = the Highs (#717–#719). What is left is R3, listed in
[`STATUS.md`](../STATUS.md#known-broken--open-follow-ups).

**The bar.** Every fix PR was **tests-first**: the new test had to fail on `origin/main` before the
fix, and the PR body says so (#717: 21 failed / 35 passed before the fix). Then code review, and a fix
for what review found, before merge — #719 went through three review rounds. Mobile PRs also had to
keep the Expo runtime fingerprint unchanged, so they ship as OTA.

### R1 — Criticals

- **Re-ingest wiped every reader's data** (data C1 + H1, #715). Re-processing a catalog book deleted
  all its chapters and inserted new ones with new Ids. `reading_progresses`, `bookmarks` and `notes`
  had `ON DELETE CASCADE` to `chapters`, highlights `SET NULL` — so every reader of that book lost
  progress and bookmarks, and highlights never painted again. Upload Retry on a Ready book did the
  same. Scratch harness on real Postgres: `origin/main` failed 10/10 checks, the branch passed 20/20.
  Chapters are now updated in place by `ChapterReconciler`, and the FKs are `NO ACTION`. #718
  finished it: positions that name a chapter by **slug** (locators, position JSON, bookmarks,
  insights) are rewritten too. Decision and limits:
  [ADR-018](../01-architecture/adr/ADR-018-reingest-updates-chapters-in-place.md).
- **Web: the last scroll was never saved** (web C1, #714). The save was a 2 s leading-edge window with
  no trailing save, and scrolling up never saved at all. Now a trailing 1.5 s debounce on every
  scroll, plus an immediate write on hide / pagehide / unmount / chapter change.
- **Web: a PUT per render** (web M4, #714). The progress hooks returned a new object on every render,
  so each render flushed a keepalive PUT. Memoized: 3 PUTs in 10 s instead of a storm.
- **Web: Next → Prev opened at the top** (web C2, #714). Restore ran while the skeleton still showed
  the old chapter (`chapterLoaded = !!chapter`), clamped to 0, and save-on-open wrote 0. Now
  `isChapterReady` (loaded *and* the identifier matches).
- **PDF Original drifted back one page per open** (web C3 #714, mobile #716). "Current page" was the
  lowest page inside a 300 px render margin, which catches the bottom of the page above. New
  `pageAtViewportTop()` in `packages/shared/src/reader/pdfPageWindow.ts`, used by web and by the
  mobile PDF viewer bundle; the margin now only decides what to render. A real 60-page PDF opened at
  page 40 four times → 40 each time (old code: 40 → 36).
- **Mobile: restore never ran on a slow network** (mobile C1, #713). If the WebView loaded before
  `getBook` answered, the book id arriving reset "WebView loaded" and no second `onLoadEnd` came — no
  restore, and every save refused for the whole visit. New pure reducer `restoreReadiness`; the book
  id now comes from the device first.
- **Mobile: "read further on another device" lost that position** (mobile C2, #713). The toast's jump
  to another chapter flushed the leaving chapter, which stamped a newer local record, so the new
  chapter opened at the top and the first scroll overwrote the other device. Now `positionHandoff`
  hands the server position to the next mount, nothing is written while leaving through the toast,
  and the jump goes through the shell's `navigateChapter` (mobile L3).

### R2 — Highs

- **Web: the chapter DOM was rebuilt on every render** (#719, found on the way to H1). In
  `ReaderSection.tsx`, `dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: … }}` built a new object each render, and
  React 19 re-set `innerHTML` whenever that object changed — on every ReaderPage render, so on every
  scroll-progress tick. That killed every live `Range`: highlights, vocabulary marks, the user's
  selection. The fix is one `useMemo` on `html`, with a test that the chapter DOM survives re-renders.
  A root cause like this makes other bugs look random.
- **Web: ghost highlights** (H1, #719). A highlight was painted wherever its text appeared, in any
  chapter. Now `findTextByAnchor` searches only the wrapper of the highlight's own chapter. **Caught in
  code review:** anchors come in three shapes — web anchors carry `chapterId`, mobile anchors are only
  `{prefix, exact, suffix}`, and an upload's server row has only `userChapterId`. The first version
  keyed on the anchor's id alone, so every highlight made on mobile would have stopped painting on
  web. The fix is `highlightChapterKey()` in `apps/web/src/lib/textAnchor.ts`: anchor id, else the
  row's `chapterId`, else `userChapterId`; a highlight with none is listed but not painted.
- **Web: font / line-height change lost the place** (H2). The anchor was read after the reflow. Now it
  is captured before, and re-applied after layout and for 1 s of resizes (webfont swap).
- **Web: bookmark on a cached chapter → 500** (H3). The offline cache keyed chapters as
  `editionId:slug` and that string was POSTed as a chapter id. Cached chapters now carry the real id,
  and a non-GUID id is never POSTed. A full offline bookmark queue was built, then **descoped to R3**
  in re-review — too much new state for one PR.
- **Web: PDF zoom / Fit / resize jumped pages** (H4). Page + fraction are captured continuously and
  re-applied before paint on scale change. At p.40: +, +, −, Fit, resize → 40 each time (old:
  33 / 28 / 60, and page 60 saved).
- **Web: `?highlight=` links** (M1). A link from the highlights drawer now holds restore and
  save-on-open until the jump lands (5 s deadline), then removes the param.
- **Mobile: PDF upload opened as text on a slow network** (H1, #717). Layout is decided on the device
  first (original file on device → Original; known non-PDF → reflow; unknown → wait), with a
  remembered `isPdf` flag. A text save of a PDF keeps the stored `page`. Trade-off: a PDF not on the
  device, behind a captive portal, shows the loader until timeout instead of the wrong layout.
- **Mobile: offline chapter changes dead-ended** (H2, M2, M9). One offline-aware `openChapter` for
  footer ‹ ›, TOC, bookmarks and highlights; an offline TOC from cached chapters.
- **Mobile: no newer-position check on return to foreground** (H3). AppState `active` re-runs it.
- **Mobile: toast** (M1). Only the action label acts; a body tap dismisses.
- **Server, same night (#718):** a progress write with no client timestamp stamps server time, and
  explain/translate cache keys carry the prompt version — see
  [Sync correctness](#2026-10-05-sync-correctness) and [Local LLM quality](#2026-10-05-local-llm-quality).

**Lessons.**
1. A parallel hunt with file:line evidence finds what users do not report: a lost position looks like
   "I must have scrolled".
2. Tests-first proves the fix touches the bug, not only the code near it.
3. Code review earns its time on *data shapes*. The three anchor shapes are in no type; only a
   reviewer who knew mobile writes anchors differently could see it.
4. Every mobile fix is unit-tested only; device checks are still owed.

<a id="2026-10-05-sync-correctness"></a>

## Sync correctness: client clock, offline highlights, three-way merge — backend, web, mobile, shared — 2026-10-05

**Why.** Architecture review items #11 and #12: silent data loss. A sync bug that drops a write and
answers 200 is the worst kind — nothing alarms, the user just finds old data.

**Progress clock (#695, #710, #718).** Catalog last-write-wins compared the client's `UpdatedAt` with
the server-clock `UpdatedAt`. A phone whose clock ran behind had its newer writes refused, with 200.
New `ProgressClock`: the client stamp (clamped to server now + 5 min) is compared only with the stored
**client** stamp, `reading_progresses.client_updated_at` (migration `ProgressClientUpdatedAt`); null
on either side → accept. #710 gave uploads the same gate (`AddUserBookProgressClientUpdatedAt`) and
put `clientUpdatedAt` in the DTOs, so clients compare like with like. #718 closed the last hole: a
write with no client timestamp (mark finished, MCP `set_book_progress`) cleared the stamp, so the next
stale queued write won; it now stamps server time. Rule: **no sync decision compares a server
timestamp with a device clock.**

**Mobile restore never waits on the network (#710).** The reader opens from the device record at once;
a background check fetches server progress after restore. Provably newer and not yet restored →
becomes the target; same chapter and the reader has not moved → silent move; otherwise one toast,
"You read further on another device". `progressRestoreOrder.test.ts` pins that the open path makes no
progress request.

**Reading sessions (#694, #695).** Web's queue flush cleared the queue first and wrote back only
failures, so sessions queued during a flush were lost; it now removes only what it settled. Mobile
dropped a session on any submit failure; it now has a persisted queue (cap 50, drop ≥ 6 days).

**Offline highlights (#694 → #708 → #709).** Three passes, each found by reviewing the one before:
1. #694: a highlight made offline stayed `pending` in IndexedDB, was never sent, and vanished on the
   next server load. New pure `planHighlightSync` merges server + local and replays creates, updates
   and deletes; a lost-response create is matched by anchor (canonical JSON — jsonb reorders keys),
   not re-POSTed.
2. #708: replaying a colour change sent `noteText: null`, which since #706 means "remove the note" —
   it erased a note written on another device. Only an edited note is sent now. Pending highlights
   also replay on sign-in.
3. #709: a real **three-way merge**. A pending edit carries its base (version, colour, note); per
   field, untouched locally → server; server still equals base → local; server changed → server. The
   PUT is version-conditional; a 409 re-plans once. Also: the bootstrap progress flush did not send
