> Postmortem: [`docs/incidents/2026-08-07-reading-position-lost-23505.md`](../incidents/2026-08-07-reading-position-lost-23505.md)

Found by Sentry on its first day in production, which is the point of having installed it. `PUT /me/progress/{editionId}` was throwing `Npgsql.PostgresException: 23505: duplicate key value violates unique constraint "ix_reading_progresses_user_id_site_id_edition_id"` — ten times in four hours, from real readers, returning a 500 and **dropping the reader's position in the book**. Nothing crashed, no test failed, and the only prior symptom would have been a user saying "it forgot where I was".

Root cause is a textbook read-then-insert with no concurrency control: `UpsertProgress` queries `ReadingProgresses` for the row, and inserts when it finds none. One reader legitimately produces overlapping writes — the 30s session heartbeat, a `sendBeacon` on unload, an offline-queue flush after reconnect, or simply a second device — so two requests both see no row, both `INSERT`, and the loser violates the unique index. The window is milliseconds wide, which is why it only appears at real traffic and never in a test.

Fix: catch the unique violation and merge into the winner's row instead of failing. On `DbUpdateException` with SQLSTATE **23505** (matched via `PostgresErrorCodes.UniqueViolation`, not a message string, so it survives locale and constraint renames) the doomed insert is detached, the winner is re-read, and the client's write is applied to it — the exact update path we would have taken had we lost the race by one more millisecond. Critically, **the stale-write guard is re-applied against the WINNER's timestamp**, so recovering from the race still cannot move a reader backwards; and the monotonic `MaxChapterNumber` high-water mark (which feeds the RAG spoiler gate) is raised through the same shared `ApplyProgressUpdate` helper used by the normal path, so the two can never drift. A unique violation with no winning row (e.g. deleted in between) is rethrown rather than silently swallowed — that is not the race we know how to recover from.

Chosen over a native `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` deliberately: the endpoint's semantics include an early return that echoes the *existing* row back when the client's timestamp is stale, which does not express cleanly in an upsert statement, and rewriting a hot path into raw SQL to fix a millisecond race is more risk than the race. The retry keeps every existing behaviour and touches one method.

**1289 unit tests green** (9 new, locking the high-water-mark monotonicity, the NULL-vs-ordinal-0 seeding rule, and that the recovery triggers on 23505 and nothing else — a foreign-key violation must still surface). Build + `dotnet format --verify-no-changes` clean. No migration.

<a id="2026-08-06-observability-sentry-for-api-worker-with-llm-agent-and-provi"></a>

## Observability — Sentry for API + Worker, with LLM agent and provider-routing spans — backend + infra — 2026-08-06

TextStack had **no error tracking at all**. OpenTelemetry has been wired since feat-0005, but it exports to an `aspire-dashboard` container that is profile-gated **off in production** — so for the whole life of the AI stack, prod telemetry has been going nowhere and nobody was alerted on anything. The trigger for fixing it was the 2026-07-14 incident (entry below): the Worker's `Ai:Routes` had no `pdf.parse` entry, so the user-PDF vision parse fell through to `Ai:DefaultProvider: ollama` and pegged the CPU-only Ollama container at **~390% for ~42 s/page across a 106-page book**. Nothing threw, nothing logged an error, no OpenAI traffic appeared, and the job simply produced zero chunks. **The failure mode was silence, and silence is exactly what a monitoring stack has to be taught to notice.** A reader of the write-up suggested emitting the resolved provider as a span attribute; that suggestion is the core of this change.

**The routing spans.** `ModelGateway.Route` and `ModelGateway.ResolvedPrimaryKey` each independently recomputed the same precedence — registry Primary → `Ai:Routes:{feature}` → `Ai:DefaultProvider` → literal `"openai"` — and neither could tell a *deliberate* route from a *silent* fallback, because the whole thing was one `??` chain that collapsed both into a string. Both now go through a single `ResolveRoute()` returning `(Key, Reason)`; the key expression is unchanged, so the resolved provider is provably identical (locked by a precedence theory in `ModelGatewayRouteReasonTests`). Every gateway call now attaches `ai.task`, `ai.provider.resolved` and `ai.provider.reason` (`route_matched` | `default_fallback`) to the ambient span — on the Sentry span *and* the OTel `Activity`, so the answer to "which model actually served this, and did anyone choose it on purpose?" comes from a trace instead of from a CPU graph. On top of that, an **expensive** task landing on `default_fallback` raises a Sentry warning outright: falling back to the default is normal and correct for most features, but for `pdf.parse`, `rag.summarize` and `podcast.script` (config `Ai:RouteAlarm:AlertOnDefaultRouteFor`) it is a defect by definition. **Throttling is load-bearing, not polish**: `pdf.parse` resolves once per page with parallelism 6, so that 106-page book would have raised 106 identical events — `AlarmThrottle` dedupes per `(task, provider, reason)` on a 60-minute cooldown (`Ai:RouteAlarm:CooldownMinutes`) while still firing the *first* hit immediately, so a bad deploy is visible within one page.

**Agent + RAG spans.** `AgentLoop.StreamAsync` is the single seam every agent runs through — Enrichment, Librarian, Tutor, StudyBuddy and the crews — because `RunAsync` is pure delegation, so one `using var trace = TraceScope.Start("agent.run", "ai.agent")` covers all of them (`using` is legal in an iterator and disposes on completion, `yield break`, throw *and* consumer abandonment). Each run reports `agent.name` (the `FeatureTag`, which is both the agent identity and the key `Ai:Routes` is indexed by, so the tag joins with routing config), `agent.model`, and measures for iterations/tokens-in/tokens-out/cost/duration; the outcome defaults to `error` and is set to `completed` or `budget_exhausted` at the three terminal paths, mapping 1:1 onto `AgentRunRecordFactory`'s statuses. `RagIndexingService` opens a `rag.index` span per book **after** the atomic claim (a lost claim is the common no-op and emits nothing) carrying `rag.kind`, `rag.book_id` and `rag.chunk_count`, and both `FailUserBookAsync`/`FailEditionAsync` — the single choke point for `rag_status = 3` — now raise a Sentry error carrying the `rag_error`. One deliberate exception: **`"indexing interrupted, retry"` is never sent**, because it fires on every deploy that lands mid-index and the sweep re-queues it — paging on a self-healing state on every release is how alerting dies.

**Provider failures.** `OllamaLlmClient` swallows non-2xx, timeouts and transport errors and returns an empty zero-token response — which is load-bearing (callers depend on it), and is precisely why those failures are invisible in `llm_traces`: `TracingDecorator` only ever sees a successful, empty answer. The swallow is untouched; each of the three branches now also calls `LlmFailureAlarm.Capture` with `ai.provider` / `ai.task` / `ai.failure` tags, on the same throttle. OpenAI failures need no such handling — they propagate to `TracingDecorator`'s two non-cancellation catches, the single choke point, which now reports them with the provider key (threaded in as a trailing optional ctor param so no existing test construction churned). Cancellation stays excluded on both paths: an SSE client disconnecting is not a model error.

**Why not `Sentry.OpenTelemetry`.** The obvious move — bridge the existing OTel spans into Sentry so there is one instrumentation — was rejected on evidence. Sentry deprecated that package, and its replacement (`Sentry.OpenTelemetry.Exporter`, on beta OTLP ingest) ships spans through the *OpenTelemetry* SDK, meaning `BeforeSend` never sees them. Our OTel spans carry `http.client_ip` (`ServiceCollectionExtensions.Hosting.cs`) and full SQL text (`SetDbStatementForText = true`), and there is no hook to strip either on the way out — that is a hard blocker for a reading app. It would also have forced Sentry's sample rate onto the shared `TracerProvider`, coupling the cost knob to the Aspire pipeline. Instead `TraceScope` dual-writes: one `Activity` (→ OTLP/Aspire, one added `AddSource` line is the *only* change to the existing pipeline) and one Sentry span (→ scrubbed at the edge).

**Noise and privacy.** `SentryScrubber` is an **allowlist, not a denylist** — every tag and extra not explicitly blessed is dropped or redacted. A denylist only stops the leaks someone remembered to enumerate; with an allowlist a future `span.SetTag("prompt", userText)` dies at the edge whether or not a reviewer caught it. On top of that: `SendDefaultPii = false`, `MaxRequestBodySize = None` (uploads are books, POST bodies are reader prompts), user identity blanked, `Authorization`/`Cookie`/`X-Admin-*` headers stripped, free text run through the existing `TraceRedactor` and truncated to 512 chars, breadcrumb `data` bags dropped wholesale, and `/health` + `/health/ready` sampled at 0.0. Client-error exceptions (`NotFoundException`, `ConflictException`, `ValidationException`, `DomainException`, `BudgetExceededException`, cancellation) are dropped — none can reach Sentry today because `ExceptionMiddleware` maps them to 4xx *without logging*, but the filter guards a future `LogError` on a validation path. Rate-limiter 429s are not exceptions and were never in scope. Sampling is deliberately asymmetric: `http.server` at `Sentry:TracesSampleRate` (0.2 prod, 1.0 dev), but `ai.agent` and `rag.index` **always at 1.0** — they are rare and they are the entire reason for this integration; sampling them at 0.2 would discard four of every five traces we added Sentry to see.

**Wiring and the no-op guarantee.** API takes `Sentry.AspNetCore` (6.8.0 ships a first-class `net10.0` TFM with zero dependencies); the Worker is a generic host with no request pipeline, so Sentry rides the ILogger provider — additive, the OTLP logging provider is untouched. Both call sites **skip the SDK entirely when `SENTRY_DSN` is empty** rather than initialising with a blank DSN (which still installs middleware and logs a startup diagnostic), so local dev, CI and forks are byte-identical to before. All the seam code (`SentrySdk.CaptureMessage`, `GetSpan`) is already inert against a disabled hub, so no `if (enabled)` guards leaked into `AgentLoop`, `ModelGateway` or `RagIndexingService`. `MinimumEventLevel = Error` is what actually captures unhandled errors: `ExceptionMiddleware` catches everything and returns a 500, so nothing ever reaches Sentry's own middleware — errors arrive via its `logger.LogError(ex, "Unhandled exception")`, confirmed in the verification below (the captured event's `logger` tag reads `Api.Middleware.ExceptionMiddleware`, `mechanism: SentryLogger`). Release plumbing is six lines of infra and no C#: `ARG GIT_SHA` → `ENV SENTRY_RELEASE` in both Dockerfiles (declared after the publish copy so a new SHA invalidates only that trivial layer, not the .NET build or the ~150 MB puppeteer install), `build.args` in compose, and `export GIT_SHA=$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)` in `deploy.yml` — `.dockerignore` excludes `.git`, so the build arg is the only way the SHA can get in, and without a release Sentry cannot do regression detection or tell you which deploy an issue started with.

**Verified end-to-end against the real DSN**, not just unit-tested: `POST /admin/diagnostics/sentry-test` (admin-gated by the existing path-based middleware; 401 unauthenticated) returned `{"eventId":"68aa5de7…","enabled":true}` and the API logged the same id; `POST /admin/diagnostics/sentry-test-error` returned 500 and produced a Sentry issue tagged **release `71c6b1da`, environment `Production`**, with `logger: Api.Middleware.ExceptionMiddleware` and only `Accept`/`Host`/`User-Agent` in the request headers — **the admin session cookie the request carried is absent**, user is empty, no body. A synthetic routing event confirmed the allowlist on the wire: `ai.task=pdf.parse`, `ai.provider.resolved=ollama`, `ai.provider.reason=default_fallback` survived; a `prompt` tag was **gone** and a `response` extra came through as `[redacted]`; an exception message containing an email arrived as `TextStack verify: synthetic failure for [redacted-email]`. One honest caveat: Sentry still shows a coarse **Geography (city-level)** derived server-side from the ingest connection IP — that is inferred at their edge, not sent in our payload, and in production it resolves to the datacenter rather than a reader. **1281 unit tests green** (35 new across `SentryBootstrapTests`, `SentryScrubberTests`, `AlarmThrottleTests`, `ModelGatewayRouteReasonTests` — including `TryEnter_RepeatedWithinCooldown_ClaimsOnce`, which replays 106 page-parallel calls and asserts exactly one event), `dotnet build` and `dotnet format --verify-no-changes` clean. No migration. No changes to web or mobile.

**Two findings from running it for real, both fixed/known before merge.** First, the scrubber leaked SQL. Pointing the local Worker at the branch build surfaced a genuine failure — the Ollama container wasn't running, so `MetadataBackfillWorker` threw 38 transport errors — and the resulting Sentry event's **breadcrumb trail carried `Executed DbCommand … SELECT m.feature_tag … FROM models`**. EF Core puts the SQL in the breadcrumb *message*, so nulling the structured `data` bag did nothing; this is the same `SetDbStatementForText` class of leak the PR avoided by rejecting Sentry's OTel exporter, quietly re-entering through the log pipeline. Fix: `ScrubBreadcrumb` now **drops EF Core command breadcrumbs entirely** (by category or by an `Executed DbCommand` message probe) rather than redacting them — a diagnostic breadcrumb is not worth shipping query text about what people are reading to a third party. Re-verified on a fresh live event: no `DbCommand`, no SQL. Second: those same 38 failures produced **exactly one Sentry event**, tagged `ai.provider=ollama`, `ai.task=bookmeta`, `ai.failure=transport` — the throttle working against a real running process rather than a unit test, and a failure mode that produced zero visibility before this PR. **To be exact about provenance, because the event itself is not:** this was a developer machine, not production — prod's `ollama` service was healthy throughout. The event nevertheless arrived tagged `environment: Production`, because the local `.env` sets `ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT=Production`. That mislabelling is a real defect in its own right (a dev box writing into the production Sentry project under a production tag) and is fixed separately; it is called out here so nobody later reads this entry as a production incident.

**What we expect it to catch first**, in order of likelihood: a `default_fallback` on `rag.summarize` or `podcast.script` in the Worker (the same class of config drift as the original incident, now audible); Ollama timeouts during vocabulary distractor generation, which are today completely invisible because the client swallows them; and terminal RAG indexing failures on user PDFs, which until now surfaced only as a book stuck on a badge in the UI.

**Owner step:** `SENTRY_DSN` must be added to the prod `.env` by hand before the first deploy that ships this — deploys go through GitHub Actions and the server `.env` is not managed by them. Until it is set, Sentry stays off in production, which is the designed safe outcome.

<a id="2026-07-18-quality-pipeline-a-shifted-double-delete-could-destroy-a-rea"></a>

## Quality pipeline — a shifted double-delete could destroy a real chapter (Ivan Ilyich lost chapter I) — infra + backend — 2026-07-18

> Postmortem: [`docs/incidents/2026-07-18-double-delete-destroyed-chapter.md`](../incidents/2026-07-18-double-delete-destroyed-chapter.md)

User report: «Смерть Ивана Ильича» (EPUB) was missing its first chapter. Diagnosed end-to-end: the extractor was **innocent** (local + prod both produced all 14 units — job `units_count=14` proved it), and the Worker saves every unit. The killer was the **quality-validation pipeline**: Claude flagged the 7-word title-page unit with TWO issues — `FRAGMENT_CHAPTER` (fix: delete, correct) and `PLACEHOLDER_TITLE` (fix: **delete**, wrong — it even carried a `suggestedTitle`) — both pointing at `chapterNumber: 1`. The apply loop sorted delete targets descending but did **not dedup**, and the internal delete endpoint renumbers remaining chapters down — so the first delete removed the title page, chapter "I" (2 510 words) slid into slot 1, and the second delete destroyed it. Three fixes: (1) the apply loop dedups delete targets (`set()`) so N issues on one chapter = one delete; (2) the validation prompt now forbids `delete` as the PLACEHOLDER_TITLE fix (a bad title is not a reason to remove content — rename with the suggested title); (3) a **server-side safety rail** in both internal delete endpoints: a chapter above **300 words** is not deletable via the quality path at all (409) — the pipeline exists to drop title pages and blank stubs, never real content. The damaged book is repaired by a re-ingestion retry after deploy. Also noted for the "skip the parser like PDF" question: the EPUB parser ran perfectly in 2 s — an epub.js original-view would not have prevented this; the failure was post-processing without a seat belt, which now has one.

<a id="2026-07-17-mobile-persistent-book-chat-parity-history-markdown-quote-an"></a>

## Mobile — persistent Book Chat parity (history, markdown, quote-and-ask, spoiler toggle) — mobile + shared — 2026-07-17

The mobile reader's Ask sheet catches up with the web Book Chat: it now runs on the persistent `/me/chat` endpoints, so **the conversation survives app restarts and is the same thread you see on web** — and mobile inherits the server-side upgrades for free (gpt-4.1 tutor answers, rolling-summary memory, your highlights as context, chapter-summary retrieval). Mobile had no SSE consumer at all (the old ask was a buffered JSON call): a new `postSse` uses **`expo/fetch`'s real `ReadableStream`** (RN's built-in fetch buffers whole bodies) with a byte-faithful port of the web SSE parser — same wire format as the legacy ask — plus a one-shot 401 refresh-retry through the existing single-flight, and a **JSON fallback on any can't-stream runtime shape** (missing `getReader`/`TextDecoder`), not just a missing body. Markdown answers render through a **contained ~180-line custom RN renderer** (headings, bold/italic, inline + fenced code, lists, blockquotes; tables degrade to a scrollable monospace block) — chosen over `react-native-markdown-display`, which is effectively unmaintained against RN 0.83/new-arch; no raw-HTML path, links stay literal text. UX parity: history loads with a skeleton, starters only on an empty thread, **Spoiler-safe** switch (subtle on your own uploads) and **Clear chat** with confirm — both optimistic with revert — and **quote-and-ask**: the selection bar gains "Ask about this", attaching the passage as a detachable quote card composed via the `chatQuote` codec, which moved to `packages/shared` (web re-exports it; web tests pass through the re-export). Citation chips + jump-to-page are unchanged; the in-flight stream aborts when the sheet closes. **Hardened by an adversarial-QA pass** (the parser port, 401-retry duplicate-safety — auth is checked before any persist — and codec move were verified sound): fixed the narrow stream-fallback, per-token re-render/scroll jank (`memo` + animate-only-on-new-turn), a stale quote/draft surviving close/reopen, ghost blank-answer turns after hard send failures, starters silently dropping an attached quote, and a table-detector false positive (pure `isTableSeparator` + tests). Accepted notes: inline `[n]` markers are plain text on mobile (the chips are the jump surface); a stream that dies without a `done` event settles as a truncated-but-finished answer (web parity). Legacy `/ask` endpoints untouched (evals/fallback). **mobile `tsc` + 70 vitest; shared 209 (8 new codec); web `tsc` + 730 vitest** through the re-export. JS-only — ships to Android via OTA; on-device musts: multi-byte glyphs across chunk splits, abort-on-close, stream jank on long answers, and the JSON degrade on a streams-less runtime.

<a id="2026-07-15-book-chat-precomputed-per-chapter-summaries-summarize-chapte"></a>

## Book Chat — precomputed per-chapter summaries: "summarize chapter N" now draws on a whole-chapter digest — backend — 2026-07-15

The deferred S2 from the chat-quality work. An overview question used to retrieve k pointwise excerpts — even at k=20 that's a sample, not a chapter. Now **indexing precomputes one summary chunk per chapter**: after the body chunks are built, `BookChunkingService` feeds each chapter's `PlainText` (12k-char clamp, ≤60 chapters) to a new **`rag.summarize`** nano route (routed in BOTH Worker and API appsettings — the Worker's Ollama default was the pdf.parse lesson) and stores a faithful 150-250-word digest as a chunk row (`is_summary=true`, text headed "Summary of Chapter N — Title:", `source_page` = the chapter's start page so the citation chip jumps there, `embedding NULL` so the existing embedder picks it up; the stamped `chunk_count` includes summaries so the Ready-flip stays consistent). **Failure-tolerant by contract**: a failed summary logs and skips — it can never fail the indexing run. Retrieval: overview-class questions fetch the book's summary rows **in the same round-trip** (spoiler-gated, user-isolated) and `MergeSummariesFirst` guarantees them into the context ahead of the RRF ranking — capped at **k/2** so body excerpts still make it in; a question that names a chapter ("summarize chapter 5") filters to **that chapter's summary** (lenient ord match spanning the 0-based catalog vs 1-based user-book numbering). Non-overview questions are byte-identical to before. **Hardened by adversarial QA**: fixed a HIGH stuck-`Indexing` deadlock (a failed summary save left `Added` entities that a later ingestion flush resurrected → physical rows > `chunk_count` → the `embedded == chunk_count` Ready-flip never fired, unrescuable by the stale sweep — now the failure path detaches them); the catalog summary gate is **strictly `<`** the reader's frontier chapter (a whole-chapter digest distils the ending — a reader mid-chapter must not get it; body excerpts keep `<=`); the k/2 cap + named-chapter filter prevent 20-summaries-zero-body answers. Deliberate notes: chapter text flows into the summarizer prompt (same accepted injection surface as `pdf.parse`); existing Ready books gain summaries only on re-index (the two prod books get a one-off reset+re-trigger at deploy); the summary path is exercised at the pure-seam level — live overview-answer quality verifies on prod. **1227 unit + 77 AiEvals** green; migration `AddChunkIsSummary` (additive, partial indexes); `dotnet format` clean.

<a id="2026-07-15-rag-deterministic-tie-breaker-on-retrieval-order-by-repo-now"></a>

## RAG — deterministic tie-breaker on retrieval ORDER BY (repo now matches the article) — backend — 2026-07-15

The published RAG-determinism article claims hybrid retrieval orders with a deterministic tie-breaker (`ORDER BY score DESC, id`) — an audit found the fix was **missing from the repo**: all four hybrid queries in `RagService` (catalog + user-book, vector + lexical) ordered on the raw score/distance alone. That's a real (if quiet) nondeterminism: `ts_rank_cd` ties are common and float-distance ties occur on duplicate chunks, so tied rows at the `LIMIT` cutoff swap between runs (Postgres heap-order dependent) → a different fused chunk set → different citations/answers for the same question and flaky `recall@k` evals. All four ORDER BYs now carry `, id`; the catalog SQL was extracted to a testable `BuildCatalogSql()` (mirroring `BuildUserBookSql`) and a new `RagServiceDeterminismTests` locks the invariant so a future edit can't silently drop it. **Deliberately NOT changed** (reported instead): the whole-table ANN scans in `HybridCatalogSearch`/`SimilarBooksService` — pgvector serves an index-assisted scan only for a pure distance ORDER BY, so adding a secondary sort key there would silently turn indexed ANN into a full scan; the per-edition/per-book RagService queries are small filtered scans where the tie-breaker is free. `RrfFusion` untouched (already deterministic — stable sort, first-seen tie order). **1185 unit + 20 search** tests green.

<a id="2026-07-15-upload-truncated-pdfs-are-caught-with-a-clear-message-instea"></a>

## Upload — truncated PDFs are caught with a clear message instead of a scary "corrupted" failure — backend + web — 2026-07-15

Prod incident, diagnosed end-to-end: a user picked a just-downloaded PDF in the upload form **while the browser was still writing it** (file birth 19:38:28 → uploaded 19:38:46 → download finished 19:38:51). The browser sent a perfectly well-formed multipart whose file part was the 16.7 MB written so far (of 24.9 MB) — valid `%PDF-` header, but no `startxref`/`%%EOF` trailer anywhere. The upload was accepted, a book row created, and ingestion later failed with *"Could not read this file. It may be corrupted or password-protected."* — technically true, actionably useless. The same class covers interrupted downloads and HTML error pages saved as `.pdf`, so it will recur. Fix at both ends, sharing one rule (`%PDF-` within the first 1 KB — tolerating junk prefixes; both `startxref` **and** `%%EOF` within the last 64 KB — generous window so real-world trailing junk never false-rejects): **server** — a pure `PdfUploadSanity.LooksLikeCompletePdf` span check (O(header+tail), no PdfPig parse) gates `UploadAsync` before any row/file is created → friendly 400 *"This PDF looks incomplete or corrupted — if you just downloaded it, wait for the download to finish and try again"*; **web** — the same check runs pre-upload via `file.slice` head/tail reads in both upload entry points (library form + homepage hero), blocking with an inline message before spending the upload; a local read glitch falls through to the server backstop rather than blocking. Clip/admin/EPUB paths untouched; mobile is covered by the server message. `dotnet build` + **1167 unit** tests (10 helper cases + a service-level reject-no-row test); web `tsc`/`vite build` + **724** vitest (13 helper + 3 new UploadForm tests).

<a id="2026-07-15-book-chat-tutor-grade-answers-prompt-rewrite-gpt-4-1-markdow"></a>

## Book Chat — tutor-grade answers: prompt rewrite, gpt-4.1, markdown rendering, 10× token headroom — backend + web — 2026-07-15

The owner put our chat side-by-side with ChatGPT on *"summarize chapter 5"* of DDIA: ChatGPT produced a structured mini-lecture (headings, bullets, an ASCII diagram); ours produced one flat paragraph. The audit found the gap was **mostly self-inflicted, not model magic**: (1) the system prompt — written for fiction companionship — literally ended with *"Keep replies short (a few sentences). No markdown, no preface."*; (2) **`MaxOutputTokens` was 400**, so a thorough answer was physically impossible; (3) `rag.ask` ran on gpt-4.1-**mini**; (4) an overview question retrieved the same top-8 pointwise chunks as a factual lookup (hence a summary citing the table-of-contents page); (5) the panel rendered plain text, so even structured output would have shown raw. Fixed all five: the prompt is rewritten for a **study-tutor register** — markdown structure encouraged (##/### headings, lists, short tables, `code`/ASCII diagrams for technical books), **adaptive length** (factual lookup → 2-4 sentences; explain/summarize/compare → one-line takeaway, structured body, short wrap-up), pedagogic style (define terms in the book's own words, walk the book's examples) — while the **strict grounding contract is preserved verbatim in intent** (excerpts are the only source of truth, every claim cites `[n]`, no outside knowledge, greeting exception, graceful miss); `MaxOutputTokens` 400→4000; `OpenAI:RagAsk:Model` → **gpt-4.1** (config wins over the Phase-12 registry, whose ModelId is display metadata — no prod promote needed); **overview-class questions** (summarize/recap/key points/"о чём глава") are detected deterministically and retrieve **k=20** instead of 8 across all three ask paths; and `ParseCitations` now accepts the grouped `[1, 2]` markers gpt-4.1 emits in structured mode. Web: assistant turns render through **react-markdown + remark-gfm** (safe-by-default, no raw HTML) with a compact theme-aware typographic scale, and inline `[n]` markers become **clickable citation sups** wired to the same page-jump as the chips (markers inside code blocks stay plain); the ~49 kB gz cost lands entirely in the already-lazy ReaderPage chunk. RAG stays the differentiator ChatGPT can't match — answers grounded in *your* file with citations that jump to the page. Deferred (noted): precomputed per-chapter summaries at index time (the real fix for whole-chapter summaries) and a gpt-5-mini shadow-route comparison via the RLOps infra. `dotnet build` + **1172 unit + 77 AiEvals** green (grounding/citation evals unchanged — the register change didn't regress them); web `tsc`/`vite build` + **714** vitest. Live before/after verified on prod post-deploy.

<a id="2026-07-15-book-chat-ask-this-book-gets-persistent-history-quote-and-as"></a>

## Book Chat — "Ask this book" gets persistent history, quote-and-ask, and your highlights as context — backend + web — 2026-07-15

The reader's AI chat grows from an ephemeral Q&A box into a **persistent, NotebookLM-style book chat** (deliberately simple: one book = one conversation — no threads, no chapter management). Previously the chat lived in React state — close the panel or reload and it evaporated; history was a client-side buffer resent per request. Now the **server owns the conversation**: new `book_conversation` (one per user+book, edition XOR user-book, unique-per-pair) + `conversation_message` (ord-ordered turns with the citations persisted as jsonb, so citation chips still jump the reader after a reload). `GET /me/chat` auto-creates/loads, `POST /me/chat/{id}/messages` streams over the **same SSE wire format** as the old ask (the web SSE parser didn't change) and appends both turns server-side — a client disconnect still persists what streamed. **Memory is bounded regardless of thread length**: the prompt gets a rolling summary (folded fire-and-forget on the nano route after every 8 unsummarized turns, 2k cap) plus the last 6 turns.

Three UX pieces ride along. **Quote-and-ask** (the ChatPDF/Claude pattern): selecting text and hitting **"Ask about this"** (which replaces the reader's Study Buddy entry — the standalone panel is unwired; the entry now works on user books too) attaches the passage as a styled quote card above the composer; under the hood it's a markdown blockquote in the plain question string, so persistence needed no schema change and reloaded history re-renders the card. **Spoiler-safe toggle**: the gate (answers only from chapters you've read) is now a visible per-conversation switch — default ON for catalog books, OFF for your own uploads; gate-off catalog chats retrieve ungated via a new `BuildUngatedAsync`. **Your highlights are chat context**: the user-book path previously sent chunks only (the catalog path already included private notes) — now your saved highlights + notes (including PDF page-anchored ones, prefixed `p.N:`) are passed as guaranteed context, so *"what did I highlight about X?"* works; ownership/site filters unit-tested against cross-user leaks. Plus **Clear chat** (resets messages + summary, keeps the toggle).

**Hardened by an adversarial-QA pass** (the ownership isolation, summary watermark math, XSS-safety, and XOR/404 surfaces were verified sound): fixed a **HIGH** ord-race (no unique index + read-then-insert meant two concurrent sends could duplicate ords and silently drop an answer — now unique `(conversation_id, ord)` + bounded 23505 retry), the first-open upsert race (23505 → re-read), a **spoiler leak-by-memory** (toggling the gate back ON now clears the rolling summary so ahead-content distilled while ungated can't resurface in gated history), dangling answerless turns when the stream dies before any delta (user turn deleted), truncated answers persisted as complete (an `[answer interrupted]` marker), a quote-codec false positive, and closed-panel streams not aborting. Legacy `/ask` endpoints untouched (mobile + evals). `dotnet build` + **1156 unit** (+ 15 chat integration) green; web `tsc`/`vite build` + **708** vitest. Live SSE/LLM + the prod smoke (history survives reload; two-device view; highlight-aware answers) verify on prod (no local OpenAI key; arm64 SIGILL). Known follow-ups: mobile still uses the stateless legacy ask (parity fast-follow); `useAsk.ts`/`StudyBuddyPanel.tsx` left orphaned for a focused diff; quiz-me / answer→note are V2.

<a id="2026-07-14-reliability-a-slow-hung-rag-parse-can-no-longer-wedge-the-in"></a>

## Reliability — a slow/hung RAG parse can no longer wedge the indexing worker — backend — 2026-07-14

> Postmortem: [`docs/incidents/2026-07-14-rag-parse-wedged-worker.md`](../incidents/2026-07-14-rag-parse-wedged-worker.md)

Follow-up to the durable-indexing work: the sweep loop `await`ed each book's chunk (vision parse) **inline**, so one slow or hung parse blocked the whole loop — no drain, and critically no stale-reclaim — until it returned. The mis-routed 74-minute Ollama parse wedged the worker exactly this way. Now the sweep **detaches** the parse to a background `Task.Run` gated by a `SemaphoreSlim(1)`: it dispatches at most one parse at a time (bounds paid vision spend) and the loop keeps running stale-reclaim + drain checks every 30 s, so a stuck parse can't wedge it. A **hard parse timeout** (`Ai:Pdf:ParseTimeoutMinutes`, default 12, clamped below the 15-min stale window) terminates a live-but-slow parse to `Failed`/"indexing timed out, retry" before the stale sweep would — and the render phase now honors the token too, not just the vision calls. Restart-safe (the atomic `rag_indexing_started_at` claim runs before the parse, so a killed detached task is recovered by the stale sweep). **Hardened by adversarial QA** — the review caught (and this fixes) a **HIGH** semaphore leak: a DB exception between `Wait(0)` and dispatch would have leaked the slot permanently → the very silent-worker-wedge the change set out to kill; now a `try/finally` releases the slot on every non-dispatched path. `dotnet build` + **1120 unit** tests green. Live parse verified on prod earlier (OSCE: 375 chunks → Ready in ~4 min on gpt-4.1); the detach/timeout paths are unit-tested where pure and reasoned through for concurrency (no local OpenAI key / arm64 SIGILL).

<a id="2026-07-14-hotfix-pdf-vision-rag-parse-routes-to-gpt-4-1-in-the-worker"></a>

## Hotfix — PDF vision-RAG parse routes to gpt-4.1 in the Worker (was Ollama) — 2026-07-14

> Postmortem: [`docs/incidents/2026-07-14-pdf-parse-fell-to-ollama.md`](../incidents/2026-07-14-pdf-parse-fell-to-ollama.md)

Regression from the durable-indexing change (which moved chunking off the API request into the Worker): the Worker's `Ai:Routes` had **no `pdf.parse` entry**, so the user-PDF vision→Markdown parse fell through to the Worker's `DefaultProvider: ollama` instead of `gpt-4.1`. On prod this pegged the CPU-only Ollama container at ~390% (≈4 cores) for the whole parse — ~42 s per page × a 106-page book — producing zero chunks visibly, no OpenAI traffic, and (because the parse is awaited inline in the sweep loop) wedging the RAG indexing worker. The API's `Ai:Routes` had `pdf.parse: openai-pdf` all along, which is why S3 worked before chunking moved processes. Fix: add `pdf.parse: "openai-pdf"` (+ the `Ai:Pdf` block) to `backend/src/Worker/appsettings.json`, mirroring the API. Config-only; Worker builds clean.

<a id="2026-07-14-reliability-ask-this-book-indexing-no-longer-gets-stuck-read"></a>

## Reliability — "Ask this book" indexing no longer gets stuck + reading sessions survive a deleted book — backend + web — 2026-07-14

Two prod-confirmed reliability holes. **"Ask this book" could stick on "Preparing… 0/0" forever.** `POST /me/books/{id}/index` (and the catalog twin) atomically claimed `NotIndexed/Failed → Indexing`, then ran the chunking — for a PDF the **minutes-long `PdfVisionParser` vision parse** — **synchronously inside the HTTP request**, only returning `202` once it finished. When that request died (Cloudflare ~60-100s timeout, or the API container restarting on deploy — which is exactly how the OSCE workbook got stuck), the row was left `Indexing` with 0 chunks and **no terminal `Failed`**; the claim only re-claims from `NotIndexed/Failed`, so `Indexing` was a permanent dead end. Fixed by moving chunking **off the HTTP path into the Worker**, mirroring the enrichment-reliability pattern: the endpoint claims and returns `202 Indexing` immediately; a `RagIndexingService` executor does the work under an atomic `rag_indexing_started_at` claim and **always reaches a terminal state** (`chunkCount==0` / exception / cancellation → `Failed`); a `RagIndexingWorker` sweep (30s, one book/cycle to bound paid vision spend) drains queued books and reclaims dead-process `Indexing` rows (`started_at` older than 15 min) → `Failed`. The `ChapterEmbeddingWorker` chunk→embed→`Ready` hand-off is unchanged; catalog and user-book paths unify on the one executor. Migration adds nullable `rag_indexing_started_at` (+ partial sweep indexes) on `user_books` + `editions`. **Hardened by adversarial QA**: the embedder now marks a book `Failed` when it permanently poisons a chunk (previously `embedded < chunk_count` stranded it `Indexing` forever, bypassing the `chunk_count=0` sweep); the executor's chunk-count stamp is a guarded `WHERE rag_status=1` UPDATE (a stale-swept `Failed` row can't be clobbered); `embedded_count` is recomputed from actual rows after chunking (was a blind `0`, losing an embedder-drains-first race); and cancellation writes "indexing interrupted, retry" instead of a misleading "no chapters". Known follow-up (accepted): full concurrency/detach for a multi-Worker deployment (the model assumes a single Worker today).

Separately, **reading sessions for a deleted book stopped flooding 500s.** After re-uploading a book (old id gone), the client's queued/`sendBeacon` sessions kept resubmitting for the dead `user_book_id`, hitting a Postgres `23503` FK violation → unhandled `DbUpdateException` → 500, retried forever. Now `ReadingSessionService.SubmitAsync` existence-checks the referenced book (owned + live user book, or a real edition) and returns **404** for a gone book (with a belt-and-suspenders `23503` catch → benign ack), and the client **prunes** the queued session on a 404 instead of re-queuing it (5xx/network still retry). `dotnet build` + **1113 unit** tests (+ session/RAG integration tests: `202 Indexing`-without-chunking, session `404`); web `tsc`/`vite build` + **684 web** vitest. The live chunk→embed→`Ready` round-trip verifies on prod (arm64 worker SIGILL + no local OpenAI key); already-stuck prod rows are cleared by a one-off backfill at deploy.

<a id="2026-07-14-reader-highlights-are-now-reachable-nav-in-reader-drawer-she"></a>

## Reader — highlights are now reachable (nav + in-reader drawer/sheet) & translation offers every language — web + mobile — 2026-07-14

Two access/UX fixes. **Highlights had no discoverable entry point** — the `HighlightsPage` route existed but nothing linked to it and there was no way to see/jump to a book's highlights while reading (acute for PDFs, where they're page-painted with no list). Added: a header **Highlights** nav link (auth-only, beside Vocabulary); a **Highlights tab in the reader's TOC drawer** (web) listing the book's highlights — color swatch + snippet + note, sorted by position (reflow text offset / PDF page+y) — where a click jumps to it (reflow scroll, or PDF `scroll-to-page`); and an **in-reader HighlightsSheet on mobile** (mirrors the bookmarks sheet, reachable from the reader bar). Chosen to fold into the existing TOC drawer rather than add a reader top-bar button (the top-bar buttons are positional for e2e). The drawer is navigation-only — recolor/delete stay in the in-page highlight popup. As part of this, the reader's **two duplicate highlight-hook instances were hoisted into one** at `ReaderPage` (it previously double-loaded highlights from IndexedDB+server in PDF mode); `useHighlightEdit` now takes the list + mutators as props. Jump-to-highlight is nonce-driven so tapping the same one re-scrolls; a highlight in an unmounted chapter navigates to its chapter then scrolls once the DOM lands; on mobile a reflow tap scrolls in place (a new `__textstackScrollToHighlight` reusing the existing anchor-range builder) instead of resetting your reading position.

Separately, the reader's **multi-word translation popup now offers the full language catalogue** (`data/languages.ts`, ~90 languages) instead of a hardcoded 16-item backend list — the single-word popup and mobile already used the catalogue, and the translation LLM handles any language, so the cap was artificial. `useTextTranslation` reads the catalogue synchronously; the backend `/translate/languages` endpoint is left in place, deprecated (no remaining caller).

**Hardened by an adversarial-QA pass** — the hoist wiring, single-load, nonce jump, sort (no-mutate), catalogue-superset defaults, and e2e-selector safety were verified sound; fixes: a `tsc`-breaking test regression (frontend build gate), the dead cross-chapter jump, the mobile position-reset, and PDF page-clamp. `dotnet` untouched; web `tsc`/`vite build` + **681 web** vitest green; **mobile `tsc`** + 52 tests. The end-to-end highlight create/paint/edit round-trip + PDF drawer-jump verify on prod / a foreground tab (arm64 SIGILL + backgrounded-canvas pause). Known follow-ups (accepted): the mobile sheet snapshots highlights at open (no live update while open); the jump nonce uses a timestamp (same-ms collision is not human-reachable).

<a id="2026-07-13-reader-highlights-work-in-the-original-layout-pdf-reader-web"></a>

## Reader — highlights work in the Original-layout PDF reader (web + mobile) — 2026-07-13

Persistent text highlights now work on **uploaded PDFs** in the Original (PDF.js) reader — create / paint / recolor / delete / note — at parity with the reflow reader, closing the ADR-012 gap where highlights were reflow-only (`liveActionsOnly` hid the button + skipped the overlay because reflow highlights anchor to DOM text ranges that don't map onto a virtualized pdf.js text layer). **Anchor model: quad-rects in PDF user-space** — a highlight stores `{ v:1, kind:"pdf", page, rects:[{x,y,w,h}], exact }` in the existing opaque `AnchorJson`, with rects captured page-relative and **unscaled** (`x=(clientRect.left−pageRect.left)/scale`), so painting (`left=x·scale`) is **scale-invariant**: it round-trips at any zoom, survives virtualization (only the ±2 mounted pages paint; re-mount repaints from state — no anchor loss), and is device-independent across reload/sync. The shared pure helper (`packages/shared/src/reader/pdfHighlightAnchor.ts` — `buildPdfAnchor`/`paintRect`/`isPdfAnchor`) is the single source of truth reused by both the web React layer and the mobile bundled vanilla-DOM viewer, so they paint pixel-identically. **No migration** — `Highlight.UserChapterId` was already nullable (`SetNull`) and `AnchorJson` already `jsonb`; the only backend change relaxes `CreateHighlight` to accept a chapterless highlight **only when the anchor is a PDF anchor** (a reflow anchor still requires a chapter — no orphans), ownership unchanged. Reflow-only consumers (`findTextByAnchor`, `getHighlightsForRange`, the chapter sort) are guarded to skip `kind:"pdf"` so a PDF anchor can never mis-anchor via its `exact` field; existing reflow/EPUB highlights are untouched, and highlights are mode-siloed (a book read in both modes keeps chapter-anchored and page-anchored highlights separate — a documented v1 limit). Painted as tinted `mix-blend-multiply` divs over the text layer (light) / `normal` blend in Dim/invert; rects are `pointer-events:none` with a **geometric hit-test** for click/tap-to-edit, so you can select text *over* an existing highlight (and on mobile a highlight tap opens the editor without toggling the immersive bars). `HighlightsPage` deep-links a PDF highlight to the reader and scrolls to its page. Notes/recolor/delete via a dedicated `PdfHighlightPopup` (web) / the existing `HighlightNoteModal` (mobile). **Hardened by an adversarial-QA pass** (the anchor math + guards were verified sound — no critical/high); fixes: select-over-highlight, orphan-anchor rejection, mobile tap double-fire, redundant note writes, and the missing guard/boundary/ownership tests. `dotnet build` + **1091 unit / 13 highlights-integration** green; web `tsc`/`vite build` + **665 web / 201 shared** vitest; **mobile `tsc`** + 52 tests; the mobile PDF-viewer bundle regenerates deterministically from `entry.ts`. Anchor math, guards, and component render are unit-tested; the pdf.js paint/zoom/virtualization visuals verify on prod / a foreground tab (arm64 worker SIGILL + backgrounded-canvas pause block a local end-to-end). Known follow-ups (accepted): multi-page selections clamp to the start page; the vocab-underline PDF layer is a cheap follow-up on the same anchor mechanism; mobile PDF create is online-only (matches existing mobile reflow); a deep-link vs resume-scroll flicker.

<a id="2026-07-13-user-books-metadata-enrichment-is-reliable-visible-descripti"></a>

## User books — metadata enrichment is reliable + visible (description/genre/year) — backend + web + mobile — 2026-07-13

Fixed a real prod hole and made background enrichment observable. **The bug:** for a book absent from Open Library (e.g. a niche uploaded PDF), the `EnrichmentAgent` (OpenAI ReAct) completes *successfully* but with confidence 0.00 and null genre/year/description — and `EnrichmentAgentMetadataGenerator.EnrichAsync` only fell back to the Ollama `BookMetadataGenerator` in its `catch` blocks (agent *error* / budget). So "succeeded-but-empty" never triggered the fallback → such books got **no description at all** (confirmed on prod: the OSCE workbook enriched to nothing while an earlier upload that happened to hit the error-fallback got a description). **Fix:** on a successful agent run with gaps (empty description, or null genre/year), `EnrichAsync` now calls the Ollama fallback and **merges field-wise — agent non-null wins, Ollama fills only the gaps**; any Ollama-sourced field nulls the returned confidence (the agent's 0.00 doesn't describe an Ollama description) so the worker's guard leaves that column honest. A fallback that throws or returns null keeps the agent's result (no cascade to a false `Failed`).

**Visible status.** Enrichment was fire-and-forget with zero UI signal — you couldn't tell if it was running, done, or had failed. Added `MetadataEnrichmentStatus` (`NotStarted/Pending/Running/Completed/Failed`) + `MetadataEnrichmentAt` on `UserBook` (migration; existing rows → `NotStarted`, badge hidden). The enrichment body moved into `UserBookEnrichmentService` with an **atomic `Pending→Running` claim** (`ExecuteUpdateAsync ... WHERE status=Pending` → loser sees rowcount 0 and bails, so the post-ingest inline-kick and the sweep can never double-run), reaching a terminal `Completed` **even when it finds nothing** (kills "forever-enriching") or `Failed` on exception. A `MetadataEnrichmentWorker` sweep (~30s) drains `Pending` (how the API's re-enrich reaches the worker) and reclaims dead-process `Running` rows older than 10 min. New `POST /me/books/{id}/enrich` (owner-scoped, rate-limited 5/min, guarded to `Ready` & not already running) re-fires *just* enrichment — no chapter re-extraction — and **short-circuits to `Completed` without a paid agent run when all fields are already present**. The detail DTO exposes the status; **web + mobile** show a subtle "Generating book details…" badge while `Pending/Running`, and "Couldn't generate details" + **Retry** on `Failed` (retry surfaces a *local dismissible* error — never the page-level one that would tear down the page — and reconciles by refetching); the page polls every 5s while enriching, capped at ~2 min. Chapter splitting (what makes a book `Ready`) is unchanged and remains the mandatory core; this only concerns the *optional* title/description/genre enrichment layer. **Hardened per an adversarial QA pass** (short-circuit vs unbounded cost, rate-limit, uniform empty-string gap detection, fallback-throw safety, page-teardown-on-retry, unbounded poll). `dotnet build` + **1090 unit** tests green (14 enrichment-merge cases); web `tsc`/`vite build` + **639 web** tests green; **mobile `tsc`** clean + 52 tests. Enrichment is agent+Ollama so end-to-end verifies on prod (no local OpenAI key; arm64 worker SIGILLs on PDF ingest). Known follow-ups (accepted): a >10 min-slow run can be reclaimed and double-run (converges, one wasted call); a PDF whose extraction *failed* has no excerpt and stays `NotStarted`; the PDF enrichment excerpt is the first extracted chapter (front-matter can weaken the description vs EPUB).

<a id="2026-07-10-extraction-drop-pdf-inline-image-extraction-for-user-books-g"></a>

## Extraction — drop PDF inline-image extraction for user books (gate, don't delete) — backend (ADR-012 S5a) — 2026-07-10

Since ADR-012, user-uploaded PDFs render **pixel-perfect via PDF.js** (they never consume the extracted chapter `<img>`) and "Ask this book" RAG **re-renders pages via vision** (`PdfVisionParser`, which doesn't read chapter images). So the #412 SMask/PDFtoImage inline-image extraction in `PdfTextExtractor` is dead weight on the user-book path — it only produced heavy PNGs and the occasional stale `<img>`. **But admin-catalog PDFs still render via reflow and DO need those inline figures**, so this is a **gate, not a delete**: added `ExtractionOptions.ExtractInlineImages` (default **false** = safe drop). When off, the per-page path skips `ExtractPageImages`/SMask compositing entirely (page = text elements only) and `Images` is `[]`; when on, today's behavior is byte-identical. **The cover is unaffected** — it always renders via `RenderFirstPageAsCover` (PDFtoImage); only the embedded-`page-1` image *selection* is inside the gate, so `Metadata.CoverImage`/`CoverMimeType` stay populated with the flag off. **Callers set intent explicitly:** admin catalog (`IngestionService`) → `true` (preserve reflow figures); user-book (`UserIngestionService`) → `false` (drop). EPUB (`EpubTextExtractor`) is untouched. **No migration, no backfill** — stale `<img>` in already-ingested `user_chapters.html` stays until a natural re-ingest/retry; the worker's `result.Images.Where(...)` loops are no-ops on empty. The image helpers (`ExtractPageImages`/`IsRecognizedImage`/SMask dedup) and the `<img>` emit in `PdfToHtmlConverter` are **kept** (live for the admin path, simply unreached when off). `dotnet build` clean; **275 extraction + 1076 unit** tests green (inverted the default `<img>`-present asserts to `<img>`-absent, added flag-on companions for the admin path, repurposed the #412 no-black-box test to "no inline images under default", proved the cover renders with the flag off); `dotnet format` clean. Part of ADR-012 S5 — multimodal figure RAG (Part B) remains deferred. (End-to-end verify is prod-only: local worker SIGILLs on arm64 and there's no local OpenAI key — a real PDF upload confirms cover renders + reader shows Original + Ask still cites pages + an admin-catalog PDF keeps its reflow images.)

<a id="2026-07-10-mobile-pdfs-render-in-original-layout-pdf-js-in-the-reader-w"></a>

## Mobile — PDFs render in "Original layout" (PDF.js in the reader WebView) — mobile + shared (ADR-012 S4) — 2026-07-10

Brings the web ADR-012 Original-PDF experience to the **mobile app**: user-uploaded PDFs now render **pixel-perfect via PDF.js inside the existing reader WebView** (canvas + selectable text layer), instead of the reflow that mangles table-heavy books. **One reader, not a fork** — `ReaderShell` branches on `source.original` (unified-reader decision), reusing the existing HTML-injection + postMessage bridge. Shipped in three slices:

- **S4a (shared foundation):** moved the pure PDF math (`buildPdfProgressPayload`/`parsePdfPageLocator` + the virtualization window helpers `computePageRings`/`resolveOpenPage`/`topVisiblePage`/`clampPage`/`dimsReadyUpTo`) from `apps/web/src/lib` to `packages/shared/src/reader` so web and the mobile viewer share one tested source; added `hasOriginalPdf` + chapter `sourceStartPage` to the shared UserBook DTOs; exported mobile `getAccessToken` + a shared `getUserBookFileUrl(id, apiBase)`.
- **S4b (viewer + selection):** a **vanilla-DOM port** of `PdfOriginalView`/`PdfPage`/`usePdfDocument` (WebView isn't React) that reuses the shared virtualization math verbatim — blob-URL **real worker** (raster off the UI thread), one-retry `getDocument`, IntersectionObserver render ±1 / keep ±2 / free-rest (`canvas.width=0`), page-dim prefetch. Bundled by a new `build-mobile-pdf.mjs` (esbuild IIFE, **legacy `es2017`** build for old Android Chromium) into `pdfViewerScript.generated.ts` (~1.75 MB inlined — flagged bundle-weight risk). Auth = **Bearer injected into pdf.js `httpHeaders`** with `baseUrl: API_ORIGIN` (same-origin lazy Range streaming → instant first paint, bounded memory; **not** byte-transfer/base64 which OOMs on a 21 MB doc). Mid-read **401 recovers silently** via the existing single-flight `onUnauthorized()` → token re-inject → reload at the same page. The DOM selection→native bridge (translate / explain / vocab / TTS) was **extracted into a shared `READER_SELECTION_BRIDGE`** so reflow + PDF share one copy (−418 duplicated lines) and fires unchanged over the pdf.js text layer.
- **S4c (progress / bookmarks / chrome):** page-based progress persists via shared `buildPdfProgressPayload` → `updateUserBookProgress` (debounced) into the **same `ProgressPercent`** field (no double-count; library card %), resume via `parsePdfPageLocator` + `resolveOpenPage` (precedence chapter `sourceStartPage` > server > 1); **page bookmarks** (`locator:"page:N"`, `chapterId:null`) with top-bar toggle + `BookmarksSheet` jump; a `PdfReaderChrome` (page-fraction bar, go-to-page input, "N / total"); **TOC → `scrollToPage(sourceStartPage)`**; a **time-only** reading session (`recordActivity`, `wordsRead=0`, never leaks page% into words); and a `pdfLoadError` overlay offering "Read as text" (reflow) when chapters exist, else a hard error.

**iOS** rides the same WebView for free. Painting persisted highlights/vocab **onto** the PDF stays deferred to S5 (anchors span the full doc; only ±2 pages are in the DOM) — selection *actions* + highlight *creation* work now. **mobile `tsc` clean; shared 190 + web 635 + mobile 52 vitest green.** **Not device-verified here** (no Android/EAS in this env; PDF.js needs a foreground WebView) — an EAS dev build must confirm first-paint, scroll fps + off-thread worker, memory (~6 live canvases on the 106 pp / 21 MB OSCE fixture), selection→translate, silent 401 recovery, progress resume, and the reflow (EPUB) no-regression from the bridge extraction. Known follow-up: the book-detail **"Continue Reading"** button opens `chapters[0]`, whose `sourceStartPage` (often 1) then wins over a saved server page — faithful to web's reader semantics; the gap is entry-point routing, not the reader. Completes ADR-012 S4; **S5** (drop now-unused inline-image extraction + multimodal figure RAG) remains.

<a id="2026-07-11-reader-hide-the-misleading-word-based-in-the-pdf-original-to"></a>

## Reader — hide the misleading word-based % in the PDF Original top bar — web — 2026-07-11

The reader top-bar progress % is word-based (`overallProgress` = words-read / total-words). But a PDF read in Original layout is a virtualized canvas — there's no per-word scroll telemetry, so its reading session is **time-only** (`wordsRead` stays 0). The consequence: the top bar showed **"0%" on page 24 of 106** — not just useless, actively misleading. Meanwhile the footer already shows the real progress (`N / total`) and library-card % is page-based (S2). Fix: a `showProgress` prop on `ReaderTopBar`, `false` in Original mode (`showProgress={!originalActive}`), so the % chip is dropped only for Original PDFs; reflow / EPUB / catalog keep their word-based %. Closes the follow-up noted in the chrome-restore fix below. `tsc` + `vite build` + ReaderTopBar tests green (a new test asserts the % renders by default and is hidden when `showProgress` is false). (The deeper trade-off — PDF reading tracks *time + page*, not *words*, so "Words read / pace" stay 0 — is inherent to showing pixel-perfect pages; deriving words-read from page-fraction × `totalWordCount` is a possible later enhancement.)

<a id="2026-07-10-reader-restore-the-standard-chrome-in-pdf-original-mode-page"></a>

## Reader — restore the standard chrome in PDF "Original" mode + page bookmarks — web + backend — 2026-07-10

Fixed a regression from ADR-012 S1: with Original (PDF.js) made the default for user PDFs, the standard reader top bar (back / bookmark / TOC / settings / search / Ask) was stuck **hidden** — immersive-mode auto-hides it and the reveal-on-tap was wired only to the reflow content (absent in Original, which scrolls internally), so it could never reappear. Users lost their bookmarks and nav. Now the top bar stays **visible** in Original (`visible={!immersiveMode || originalActive}`, pinned via a `.reader-page--original` CSS override; the PDF's own Fit/zoom/page/Dim toolbar sits below it). Controls are adapted to the PDF: **page-based bookmarks** — the bookmark button toggles the current PDF page (stored as `locator:"page:N"`), the TOC drawer lists them as "Page N" and clicking one jumps the viewer (`setPdfScrollTo`); chapter TOC entries still jump to their `sourceStartPage`. Typography settings (font size / line height / align / family) are hidden in Original (they don't apply to a canvas; Theme/TTS/inline-translation stay). Search is hidden in Original (the reflow-DOM overlay doesn't apply — page-aware in-book search is a follow-up) rather than shipping a no-op button. Back / Ask / TOC / the S3 citation→page jump all work with the restored bar. `PdfOriginalView` now surfaces the current page (`onPageChange`). Backend: `UserBookBookmark.ChapterId` made **nullable** (migration `AddUserBookmarkNullableChapter`, FK → SET NULL) so a page bookmark needs no chapter; the create request/DTO accept a null chapter. `dotnet build` + **1076 unit** + **662 web** tests green (23 new); web `tsc` + `vite build` clean; browser-verified the top bar is visible in Original with bookmark/TOC/settings/Ask (search hidden). Known minor: the top-bar % still reads word-based (0% for a chapterless PDF) — page-based top-bar % is a follow-up.

<a id="2026-07-10-ai-quality-pdfvision-eval-gate-scores-the-pdf-vision-rag-fea"></a>

## AI quality — `pdfvision` eval gate scores the PDF vision-RAG feature end-to-end (ADR-012 S3) — backend — 2026-07-10

Added an admin-runnable eval gate that scores the ADR-012 S3 vision-RAG pipeline on **four axes** without a seeded book or DB pollution — a self-contained in-process run over **synthetic, original table-page fixtures** (no copyrighted content). New endpoint `POST /admin/ai-quality/evals/pdfvision/run?judge=openai|ollama` → `PdfVisionEvalRunner` (mirrors `UserBookRagEvalRunner`, incl. the empty-transcription short-circuit + multi-row persist). **The gate exercises the exact prod seams:** transcribe each fixture page through the gateway `pdf.parse` route (→ gpt-4.1) with a **shared `PdfVisionPrompt`** now extracted from `PdfVisionParser` (prompt-parity — the eval can never drift from what prod ships) → LLM-as-judge fidelity of the page Markdown vs golden table facts (`pdfvision.transcription`, 1–5, ≥4.0) → the production `MarkdownChunker` with a deterministic "every golden table's rows land in ONE chunk" check (`pdfvision.tablestructure`, =1.0) → embed chunks + questions, cosine top-k, **stamp `SourcePage` exactly as `RagService` does**, then the real `IRagAskService.AskFromChunksAsync` → answer-fidelity judge vs expected facts (`pdfvision.answer`, 1–5, ≥4.0) and a deterministic "the answer cited the expected page" fraction (`pdfvision.citation`, ≥0.8). Persists four `pdfvision.*` `EvalRun` rows (the Evals tab renders them by feature; **not** added to the scheduled `ContinuousEvalWorker`). **Fixtures:** three original medical-flavored tables ("Corneal Ulcer", "Preseptal vs Orbital Cellulitis", "Acute Angle-Closure Glaucoma") authored for this gate, rendered to JPEG and committed as embedded resources — the vision model transcribes a real table image, with a `pdfvision.json` golden of per-page fact substrings + 6 Q&A whose answers live only in a table cell. **CI deterministic half** (`PdfVisionEvalRunnerTests`, 5 tests, no key): fakes prove table-survives-whole = 1.0, the page-citation fraction math, the 4-row persistence, and that an empty transcription short-circuits with no judge call; the live-scored half runs on prod (real key). `dotnet build` + **82 AiEvals** (77 pass / 5 keyless-skip) green; `dotnet format` clean. Scope: no S3 runtime behavior change beyond the prompt-parity extraction; no live vision call locally.

<a id="2026-07-10-ask-this-book-vision-rag-for-pdfs-with-page-citations-that-j"></a>

## Ask this book — vision-RAG for PDFs with page citations that jump to the page (ADR-012 S3) — backend + web — 2026-07-10

"Ask this book" now builds its RAG index for user **PDFs** from a **gpt-4.1 vision → clean Markdown** parse instead of the deterministic text extraction that jumbles tables — so answers over table-heavy books (the OSCE workbook) are grounded in coherent, structured content — and every answer citation carries a **page number** that **jumps the Original PDF viewer to that page**. Runs lazily inside the existing on-demand index (first "Prepare"/Ask), cached; EPUB keeps deterministic chunking. **Pipeline:** a new multimodal seam (`LlmMessage.Images` + `OpenAiLlmClient` image content part — every call still flows through `ILlmService` for tracing/routing/budget) → `PdfVisionParser` (Infrastructure; renders pages via PDFtoImage at 150 DPI sequentially — PDFium isn't thread-safe — then transcribes with bounded parallelism 6 on the `pdf.parse` route → `gpt-4.1`) → a structure-aware `MarkdownChunker` (splits on headings with a section-path stack, **never splits a table mid-row**, ~512-token cap) → chunks carrying `SourcePage` + `SectionPath`, attached to the book (page→chapter mapped via S2 `SourceStartPage/EndPage` when available, else null). Retrieval + the Ask prompt surface the page (`[n] (p.17 · 2. Orbit › Infectious) …`), so the LLM cites "p. 17"; the citation chip shows "p. N" and, in Original mode, routes to the existing `setPdfScrollTo` jump instead of the reflow scroll. **Guards:** page cap (`Ai:Pdf:MaxParsePages`=300 → partial + warn), the per-feature daily USD budget (`Ai:Budgets:Features:pdf.parse` — also fixed `gpt-4.1`/`gpt-4.1-mini` being priced at 0, which had made the budget guard a silent no-op), and graceful fallback to the deterministic chunker on any vision/render/budget failure. **Schema:** migration `AddUserChapterChunkProvenance` — `user_chapter_chunk.source_page`/`section_path` (nullable, safe) and `user_chapter_id` made nullable (FK → SET NULL) since PDF chunks are page-anchored, not chapter-anchored; per-user isolation (`user_id + user_book_id`) is unchanged. `dotnet build` + **1068 unit + 72 AiEvals** + **651 web** tests green; web + **mobile `tsc`** clean; `vite build` green (pdfjs stays lazy). **No live vision run** — there's no valid OpenAI key locally; vision transcription quality + the end-to-end chat lift verify on prod (real key). Completes the ADR-012 core (S1 instant-read, S2 page-progress, S3 vision-RAG); S4 (mobile Original) + S5 (cleanup / multimodal figures) remain.

<a id="2026-07-10-reader-pdf-reading-progress-is-page-based-library-resume-web"></a>

## Reader — PDF reading progress is page-based (library % + resume) — web + backend (ADR-012 S2) — 2026-07-10

User PDFs opened in "Original layout" now record a **page-based** reading position server-side, so the library card shows a real progress % and reading resumes to the right page across devices. `PdfOriginalView` debounces the top visible page both to localStorage (offline) and to the server (`PUT /me/books/{id}/progress` with `{chapterSlug:null, locator:"page:<N>", percent: page/numPages}`), flushing on `visibilitychange:hidden`/unmount. On open, resume precedence is: the chapter's `sourceStartPage` (when navigating to a specific chapter) > server page > localStorage > 1, and the initial scroll waits for the server answer so a cross-device open lands correctly. The page fraction goes into the **same `ProgressPercent` field** the reflow/word path uses — no double-count; the library card + the (R6-fixed) shelf read it verbatim. Backend: `UpsertUserBookProgressRequest.ChapterSlug` is now nullable and `GetProgressAsync` treats a `page:N` locator (not just a chapter slug) as "progress exists" — **no migration** (`ProgressChapterSlug` was already nullable). Library card: the progress bar/`% read` and the read-link now gate on `readable` (`isReady || hasOriginalPdf`) so a still-`Indexing` PDF shows its page-%. EPUB/catalog word-based progress and mobile untouched. `tsc` clean; `dotnet build` + **646 web + backend unit** tests green (16 new). Part of ADR-012. (Known minor: the unmount flush uses a non-`keepalive` fetch — `visibilitychange` lands it in practice; a keepalive variant is a later polish.)

<a id="2026-07-10-reader-pdfs-open-instantly-original-only-adr-012-s1-web-back"></a>

## Reader — PDFs open instantly, Original-only (ADR-012 S1) — web + backend — 2026-07-10

User-uploaded **PDFs now open the instant the file is stored** — no more waiting for extraction before reading — and render in **Original layout by default** (the reflow toggle is gone). Readability is *derived*: a book is readable as soon as it has a stored PDF (`hasOriginalPdf`), independent of extraction status. `Status==='Processing'` on a PDF is now a quiet "Indexing…" badge, not a blocker; `Status==='Failed'` on a PDF (extraction failed but the file opens fine in PDF.js) no longer shows a scary "Processing failed" block or dead-links the card — it just means text features (search/TOC/chat) are unavailable. **The load-bearing change:** the reader was hard-coupled to a chapter (it errored on `!chapter`); it's now decoupled — a new chapterless route `/:lang/library/my/:id/read` (no slug) opens straight into the PDF with `chapter=null`, and `useReaderChapter` returns `{book, chapter:null}` for a slugless open or a chapter-404 when `hasOriginalPdf` (EPUB 404s stay fatal). On a successful PDF upload the modal redirects straight into the Original reader (instant read). **Fallback:** a hard PDF.js open failure falls back to reflow if chapters exist, else a dedicated "Couldn't open this PDF" screen (re-upload/retry) — the internal 401 session-expired reload path is untouched. Backend: `HasOriginalPdf` added to the list DTO + upload response (derived from stored `BookFile.Format==Pdf`) — **no migration, no status-enum, no worker change**. EPUB (parse-then-reflow), the admin catalog, and mobile are untouched (mobile Original is S4). `tsc` clean; `dotnet build` + 635 web + backend unit tests green; browser-verified the chapterless route opens Original with no error and no toggle. Owed: foreground visual of canvas paint + live upload-to-instant-read. Part of ADR-012 (original-first PDF, parsing decoupled & lazy for AI).

<a id="2026-07-10-ops-root-cause-the-nightly-backup-was-leaking-156-gb-of-orph"></a>

## Ops — root cause: the nightly backup was leaking 156 GB of orphaned pgdata volumes — infra — 2026-07-10

> Postmortem: [`docs/incidents/2026-07-10-backup-leaked-156gb.md`](../incidents/2026-07-10-backup-leaked-156gb.md)

The actual root cause behind the flapping backup (the self-diagnostics below were added to *find* this). Prod's Docker data-root lives on a **separate `/mnt/data` partition** (not `/`, which `df -h /` alone hides) and it had hit **100% / 0 bytes free** — 183 GB used, **156 GB of it 57 dangling anonymous `pgdata` volumes**. `backup-verify.sh` spins a throwaway `pgvector` postgres each run; postgres declares an anonymous `VOLUME /var/lib/postgresql/data`. On a killed/timed-out run `--rm` never fired and the cleanup did `docker rm -f` **without `-v`** → one full pgdata volume leaked per run. 34 days × daily ≈ 156 GB → the docker root filled → a fresh throwaway postgres could no longer `initdb` → **the backup broke the very verify step that had been creating the volumes** — a self-reinforcing failure. Real prod data was never at risk (postgres + storage bind-mount to `/`, which was at 61%); only the anonymous verify volumes bloated `/mnt/data`. Fix: `docker rm -f` → **`docker rm -fv`** (both the trap cleanup and the leaked-container reap) so the anonymous volume dies with its container, plus a `docker volume prune -f` catch-all for SIGKILL'd runs the trap can't cover (named volumes are untouched by prune). Remediated live: `docker volume prune` reclaimed **163 GB**, `/mnt/data` 100% → 17%. Diagnosed the hard way — the box was also unreachable for a stretch (a clean manual reboot, not a crash; a full disk still lets you SSH in, so an SSH *connect*-timeout is a host-offline signal, not disk).

<a id="2026-07-10-ops-backup-verify-self-diagnoses-failures-reaps-leaked-conta"></a>

## Ops — backup verify self-diagnoses failures + reaps leaked containers — infra — 2026-07-10

The daily backup's restore-verify step (`infra/scripts/backup-verify.sh`, which restores the dump into a throwaway pgvector container) was failing with a bare `postgres did not become ready` that hid the real cause. Hardened it: (1) reap leaked `textstack_backup_verify_*` containers from prior killed/timed-out runs before starting (they hold ports + memory and can starve the fresh postgres); (2) on readiness failure, dump the container's `docker logs`, `df -h`, and `docker system df` into the CI log — a full disk (initdb can't write its data dir) is the usual culprit; (3) bail early if the container exits during startup instead of waiting the full window; (4) bump the readiness timeout 30→60s. The DB dump + storage tar.gz themselves were always succeeding — only the verify gate flapped. Tested end-to-end against a real 729 MB dump: restore + sanity queries pass. Root-cause disk cleanup on the server (if confirmed) is a follow-up.

<a id="2026-07-10-reader-original-layout-pixel-perfect-pdf-view-for-uploaded-b"></a>

## Reader — "Original layout": pixel-perfect PDF view for uploaded books — web + backend — 2026-07-10

Added an opt-in **"Original layout"** mode to the reader that renders a user-uploaded book's ORIGINAL PDF pixel-perfect (via PDF.js: page canvas + transparent selectable text layer), alongside the default reflow reader. Reflow stays default (it powers TTS/resize/dark-mode/vocab — the learning engine); Original is for style-heavy books (tables, colored section pills, red keywords) where fixed layout matters and reflow inevitably discards it. Because the text layer is real DOM, the existing selection pipeline works unchanged over the PDF — translate / explain / add-to-vocabulary / TTS / copy all fire (`liveActionsOnly`: persistent highlight + vocab-underline overlays are reflow-only v1; vocab still saves to SRS). Toggle lives in the reader top bar, shown only for user-uploaded PDFs (`hasOriginalPdf`), remembered per-book. Opening a chapter jumps the PDF to that chapter's page; the chapter TOC scroll-jumps within the PDF. Canonical word-based progress is never touched by PDF page position (page position is localStorage resume-only); reading TIME still counts toward streak/goals via a throttled activity ping. **Backend:** new `GET /me/books/{id}/file` streams the stored original with HTTP Range (206/`Accept-Ranges`, strong sha256 ETag, owner + `TakedownAt` + PDF-only enforced in the query); `UserBookDetail.hasOriginalPdf`; new `UserChapter.SourceStartPage/EndPage` (migration `AddUserChapterSourcePages`) threaded from the extractor's chapter page-range so the reader can jump to a chapter's physical page. **Frontend:** `pdfjs-dist@4.10.38` (lazy async chunk — 373 KB + worker, loaded only when Original opens; per-document worker so lifecycle teardown never strands a reload), `PdfOriginalView` (virtualized page list — bounded ~6 live canvases for a 100-page/21 MB PDF — toolbar: fit/zoom/page-jump/dim, session-expired reload recovery), `PdfPage`, `usePdfDocument`. `tsc` clean, `vite build` green (pdfjs stays out of the entry chunk), **631 web + backend extraction/integration/unit** tests green (39 new incl. range-endpoint isolation/takedown/ETag, scroll-window math, per-book toggle, canvas lifecycle, liveActionsOnly gating, per-document worker teardown). Browser-verified: pixel-perfect render (pink pills / tables / red keywords), correct chapter-page open, page-jump, selectable text → toolbar. **Deferred:** persistent PDF-coordinate highlights; mobile WebView Original mode.

<a id="2026-07-09-auth-longer-sessions-refresh-token-ttl-30-365-days-backend"></a>

## Auth — longer sessions: refresh-token TTL 30 → 365 days — backend — 2026-07-09

Raised the signed-in session window so users stay logged in until they explicitly log out (or are inactive a full year) rather than being dropped after 30 days. `Jwt:RefreshTokenExpiryDays` 30 → **365** in `appsettings.json` (drives the refresh-token row `ExpiresAt`), and `SetAuthCookies` now sets the auth-cookie `MaxAge` from that same `JwtSettings` value instead of a hardcoded `TimeSpan.FromDays(30)` — otherwise the browser would drop the cookie at 30 days even though the token is valid for a year. Both the token and the cookie are re-issued on every `/auth/refresh`, so an active reader's window slides forward continuously; combined with the single-flight refresh fix below, an active user effectively never gets logged out. Guest sessions unchanged (`GuestRefreshTokenExpiryDays` stays 30 to bound abandoned-account DB rows). Note: the admin "Session settings" DB knob only affects the *admin* cookie MaxAge — regular-user token TTL is driven by `JwtSettings` (appsettings), so this is the effective lever. `dotnet build` green.

<a id="2026-07-09-auth-fix-spurious-unauthorized-mid-session-refresh-token-sta"></a>

## Auth — fix spurious "Unauthorized" mid-session (refresh-token stampede) — web — 2026-07-09

> Postmortem: [`docs/incidents/2026-07-09-refresh-token-stampede.md`](../incidents/2026-07-09-refresh-token-stampede.md)

Fixed the reader dead-ending on **"Error loading chapter — Unauthorized"** after ~an hour, even though the user was still signed in (Google/email). Root cause: the access token expires at 60 min, and the reader fires many authenticated requests in parallel (chapter, book, progress, bookmarks…), so they all 401 at the same instant and each independently called `/auth/refresh`. The server **rotates** the refresh token on every refresh (deletes the presented token, issues a new one), so only the first concurrent refresh won — the rest presented the now-deleted token and got 401, surfacing as a spurious mid-session logout. Fix: `refreshToken()` in `apps/web/src/api/auth.ts` is now **single-flight** — concurrent callers share one in-flight `/auth/refresh`, so there's exactly one rotation and one fresh cookie, then every caller retries. This makes silent refresh reliable, so an active user stays signed in across the full rotating refresh-token window (i.e. until they explicitly log out or are inactive past the TTL) instead of being bounced hourly. Pure client change, no backend/token-config change. `tsc` clean, `vite build` green, **22 auth/api vitest** green (3 new: concurrent-dedup, fresh-request-after-settle, lock-clears-on-failure). Mobile (`apps/mobile/src/lib/api.ts`) already single-flights refresh — not affected.

<a id="2026-07-09-library-drop-the-shelf-carousels-book-grid-to-the-top-web"></a>

## Library — drop the shelf carousels, book grid to the top — web — 2026-07-09

Removed the `LibraryShelves` carousel block (Continue reading, Recently added, Quick reads, Finished this month) from the library page — the shelves were buggy and low-value, and pushed the actual book list below the fold. The unified book grid/list (sort · search · status tabs · cards) now sits directly under the stats header, so opening the library lands you on your books. One-line change in `apps/web/src/pages/LibraryPage.tsx` (drop `<LibraryShelves>` + its import); the grid's own empty-state (`EmptyState`/`UploadDropZone`) covers the no-books case the shelves block used to handle. The `/library/shelf/:shelfId` "View all" route + `LibraryShelvesService`/`useLibraryShelves` stay intact (reachable by direct URL) — no backend change, trivially reversible. `tsc` clean, `vite build` green, browser-checked (shelves gone, no "Continue reading"/"Recently added" text, grid renders at top, 0 console errors).

<a id="2026-07-09-pdf-reader-fixes-word-quartz-exports-parse-cleanly-kmk-osce"></a>

## PDF reader fixes — Word/Quartz exports parse cleanly (KMK OSCE model book) — extraction + web/mobile — 2026-07-09

Fixes a customer complaint about a user-uploaded PDF (`KMK Optometry OSCE E-Workbook.pdf`, a Word→macOS-Quartz export) that read terribly in the web reader: no real chapters (just "Pages 1–15" … "Pages 106–106"), ugly URLs (`/read/1-pages-115`), black/empty image boxes, and a progress bar that jumped. Adopted the book as a **test fixture** so these Word/Quartz cases stay fixed. Four deterministic root-cause fixes: **(A) Images** — `page.GetImages()` returns Quartz's stacked base+SMask companions as separate XObjects, and every image ≥2 KB was inlined → black-box + empty-box pairs. `PdfTextExtractor.ExtractPageImages` now dedups same-bounding-box stacks (keep the real base), suppresses full-page background/divider rasters, and drops degenerate 1×1/fully-transparent overlays + sub-48pt annotation labels (PdfPig's `TryGetPng` already composites `/SMask` alpha correctly, so no re-rasterization needed). Verified on the live book: 153 raw XObjects → **38 inlined images, 0 black/empty boxes**. **(B) Chapters** — `PdfChapterDetector` gained two levels between bookmarks and the 15-page-chunk fallback: a **TOC-anchored** parser (`PdfTocParser`, reusing `FrontMatterFilter.TocLeaderLine` to read the leader-dotted ToC, then anchoring each title to its first body page — sidesteps printed-vs-physical page offset) and an extended **heading heuristic** (numbered/roman/`PART`/ALL-CAPS, not just "Chapter N"). The book now yields **23 real-titled chapters** ("Urgent and Emergent Eye Care", "Corneal Ulcer", …) → clean slugs, no more `pages-115` URLs. **(C) Title** — `BookTitleCleaner` strips the `Microsoft Word - ` prefix, `.docx/.doc/.pdf/…` extension, and ` copy N` suffix: "Microsoft Word - OSCE Tables 23+24 FINAL copy 5.3.23.docx" → "OSCE Tables 23+24 FINAL". **(D) Progress** — root-caused a structural bug: `UserBook.ProgressPercent` was written as a **book-wide %** by the web reader but the "Continue reading" shelf (`LibraryShelvesService`/`BookProgressCalculator`) re-added prior-chapter words as if it were a *chapter* %, so the library card and the shelf showed different numbers for the same book (the "jumps"). Canonicalized `ProgressPercent` = book-wide everywhere: the shelf now reads the stored user-book value verbatim (card == shelf, no double-count), mobile's `buildUserBookProgressPayload` writes a book-wide % via the shared `computeBookProgress` (was chapter-%), the web reader passes `totalWordCount` for a single denominator, and `UserIngestionService` numbers saved chapters densely 1..N (Contents no longer starts at "2" after the ToC chapter is filtered). Side fix: `SlugGenerator` maps en/em-dash → `-` so ranges don't fuse ("1–15" → "1-15"). Builds green; **267 extraction + 1040 unit + 89 shared-reader** tests green (new fixture-driven chapter/image/title tests + a card-vs-shelf no-double-count test); web `tsc` clean. **Out of scope, flagged for follow-up:** the public-edition reader writes book-wide % into `ReadingProgress.Percent` too, so the edition shelf path has the same latent double-count (user-books-first this PR). **Deferred (PR2):** optional AI document-QA gate (vision check for black boxes / LLM chapter-structure validation).
